## W177. Vending & Concessionaire Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New concessionaire application; monthly rent/commission calculation |
| **Frequency** | ~1–3 new concessionaire onboardings per store per year; monthly billing cycle |
| **Volume** | ~200–600 active concessionaire agreements chain-wide (1–3 per store across 200 stores) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Concessionaire, Finance (AR), Store Operations |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Onboarding**: Store Manager reviews application for in-store cafe, snack bar, or ATM space; validates compliance with safety/health standards | Store Mgr | — | 2 hours |
| 2 | **Contract Setup**: Legal/Finance setup lease in ERP (W117); defines Rent or % Commission on Sales | Finance | — | 1 hour |
| 3 | **Sales Tracking**: If commission-based: Concessionaire sales are rung through a dedicated "Non-Inventory" SKU at POS; system tracks total monthly throughput | Cashier | — | Continuous |
| 4 | **Billing**: Monthly: Finance generates invoice for Rent + Utilities + Commission; offsets any payments collected on behalf of concessionaire | AR Clerk | Controller | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Lease management integration (W117)
- Non-inventory POS SKU for concessionaire sales tracking
- Automated monthly billing

### Pain Points / Risks
- Concessionaire sales underreporting — commission-based concessionaires may under-ring sales or process transactions outside the dedicated POS SKU, reducing BuildRight's commission revenue
- Health and safety liability — food concessionaires (cafes, snack bars) must comply with LGU health permits and food safety standards; any violation reflects on BuildRight's store reputation and may trigger LGU inspection of the entire premises
- Utility cost allocation disputes — concessionaires consuming disproportionate electricity or water relative to their leased space, with no sub-metering to accurately allocate costs
- Concessionaire abandonment — operator ceases business but leaves equipment and contractual obligations unresolved, requiring legal intervention per W62 contract termination


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews application for in-store cafe, snack bar, or ATM space" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-track of "tracking" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "generates invoice for Rent + Utilities + Commission" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Store Manager review and approval gate

### Time Estimate
~2 hours per new concessionaire onboarding + 1 hour contract setup. Ongoing: ~30 min/month for Finance billing and reconciliation per concessionaire. Store Manager oversight: ~15 min/month per concessionaire. With an estimated 1–3 concessionaires per store across 200 stores, total ongoing management is ~100–300 hours/month chain-wide.

### Staffing Implication
- Store Manager: ~15 min/month per concessionaire for oversight and coordination. With 1–3 per store, this is ~15–45 min/month. Absorbed.
- Finance (AR Clerk): ~30 min/month per concessionaire for billing and reconciliation. Across 200–600 agreements chain-wide, this is ~100–300 hours/month. Absorbed within existing AR team.
- No incremental headcount. Onboarding events (1–3 per store per year) handled by existing Store Manager and Legal/Finance teams.

## W182. Gift / Home Registry Lifecycle

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (Home Builder/Renovator) creates a "Project Wishlist" or "Registry" |
| **Frequency** | ~50–100 new registries per month |
| **Volume** | Average registry value PHP 20,000–100,000; 10–30 items per registry |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Rep (CSR) |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Gift Givers |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Creation**: Customer creates Registry via Website or In-store Tablet; specifies "Housewarming" or "Renovation Project" | Customer | — | 15 min |
| 2 | **Item Selection**: Customer walks store with Sales Associate; scans items into Registry using Mobile App | Customer / Assoc | — | 1 hour |
| 3 | **Sharing**: Customer shares Registry link with family/friends; or provides "Registry ID" for in-store lookup | Customer | — | — |
| 4 | **Purchase**: Giver buys item from Registry (In-store or Online); system marks item as "Purchased" to prevent duplicates | Cashier / System | — | 2 min |
| 5 | **Fulfillment**: Items can be picked up by Giver, or held at CSR counter for "Consolidated Delivery" to the Registry Owner | CSR | Store Mgr | 10 min |
| 6 | **Completion**: Registry Owner receives "Completion Discount" (e.g., 10% off remaining items) after the event date | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Registry master data (Customer + Items + Event Date)
- Real-time "Purchased" status sync across channels
- Completion discount logic (Promo engine integration)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Low registry completion rate — Philippine hardware/home improvement registries are a nascent concept; most gift givers prefer cash gifts, leading to registries with few purchased items and disappointed registry owners
- Inventory availability changes during the registry lifecycle — items selected into the registry may go out of stock or be discontinued (W68) before gift givers purchase them, requiring CSR outreach to update the registry
- Consolidated delivery logistics complexity — holding purchased items at the CSR counter for a consolidated delivery to the registry owner requires dedicated staging space and coordination, competing with layaway and BOPIS staging areas
- Completion discount margin erosion — offering 10% off remaining items after the event may cannibalize full-margin sales if customers create registries primarily to access the completion discount rather than for genuine gift coordination


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-create of "creates Registry via Website or In-store Tablet" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scans items into Registry using Mobile App" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "counter" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate
~1 hour 15 min per registry creation (15 min setup + 1 hour item selection walk). Ongoing per-registry maintenance: ~10 min. At 50–100 new registries/month chain-wide, total CSR and Sales Associate time is ~60–125 hours/month. Gift giver purchases are standard POS transactions (~3 min each).

### Staffing Implication
- CSR: ~1 hour 15 min per registry creation. At ~0.25–0.5 new registries per store per month, total CSR time is ~30–60 min/store/month. Absorbed.
- Sales Associate: 1 hour per registry item selection walk. Rotational duty — absorbed within existing floor staff.
- No incremental headcount. Registry volume is low enough to be handled as an additional CSR service alongside returns and layaway.

## W212. Automated Store Cash Management & Smart Safe Integration

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Shift end; or cash drawer limit reached |
| **Frequency** | 2–3 times per day per cashier |
| **Volume** | Covers all 200 stores; ~600 POS terminals |
| **Owner** | Store Cashier Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Supervisor, CIT (Cash-in-Transit) Partner |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Drawer Limit Alert**: System triggers alert when POS cash drawer exceeds threshold (e.g., PHP 50,000) | System | Cashier | Real-time |
| 2 | **Cash Skimming**: Cashier counts excess cash; Supervisor verifies; records "Skim" in POS | Cashier | Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | **Smart Safe Deposit**: Cashier inserts bills into the in-store Smart Safe; safe validates bills and counts in real-time | Cashier | — | 3 min |
| 4 | **Credit Recognition**: Smart Safe API sends deposit confirmation to ERP/Bank; Bank provides "Same-Day Credit" to BuildRight's account | System | Finance | Automated |
| 5 | **EOD Reconciliation**: At EOD, system reconciles POS Sales vs. Smart Safe Deposits vs. Remaining Drawer Cash; flags variances | System | Supervisor | Automated |
| 6 | **CIT Pickup**: CIT partner (e.g., G4S/Securicor) empties safe when full; system logs pickup; safe resets for next cycle | CIT Partner | — | 20 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Smart Safe API integration with ERP and Bank (W30)
- POS cash limit alerts and skimming workflow
- Automated daily bank reconciliation for cash deposits
- CIT pickup tracking and safe capacity monitoring

### Pain Points / Risks
- Smart Safe hardware failure — if the safe's bill validator jams or the API connection to the bank fails, cash cannot be deposited, and same-day credit is lost, forcing manual cash handling until repair
- Skimming count discrepancies — cashier and supervisor counts disagree on the excess amount, causing delays and disputes that require Store Manager intervention and CCTV review per W272
- CIT pickup scheduling rigidity — armored car partners operate on fixed schedules; if a store's safe reaches capacity between scheduled pickups, cash must be held overnight in the manual safe, increasing security risk
- Bank credit timing dependency — same-day credit relies on the smart safe deposit being processed before the bank's cutoff time (typically 3:00 PM for Philippine banks); late-day deposits receive next-day credit, affecting daily cash position reporting for Treasury


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-alert of "Drawer Limit Alert" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-record of "records" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "validates bills and counts in real-time" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Time Estimate
~8 min per skim event (5 min count/verify + 3 min smart safe deposit). At 2–3 times per cashier per day × 3 terminals = 6–9 skim events per store per day = ~50–70 min/store/day. EOD reconciliation: automated. CIT pickup: 20 min per visit (typically 1–2 times/week). Chain-wide daily skim labor: ~170–230 hours/day.

### Staffing Implication
- Cashier Supervisor handles 6–9 skim events per store per day (~50–110 min, per the 2–3 skims/terminal × 3 terminals derivation). Absorbed into existing supervisory duties.
- CIT partner visits 1–2 times/week per store (20 min each). No incremental BuildRight headcount.
- Smart Safe hardware is a per-store capex investment (~PHP 150K–300K per unit). Justified by reduced cash handling risk and faster bank credit.

---

## W278. Mid-Day Cash Skimming / Till Sweeps

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | A POS till exceeds the maximum allowable cash holding threshold (e.g., PHP 50,000) during trading hours. |
| **Frequency** | Daily, multiple times per store |
| **Volume** | 2-5 skims per POS terminal per day |
| **Owner** | Store Cashier Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Vault Custodian / Supervisor |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | POS system alerts Cashier and Supervisor that the till has exceeded the PHP 50,000 cash holding threshold | System | Cashier Supervisor | 5 min |
| 2 | Supervisor initiates a "Cash Drop / Skim" transaction on the POS terminal without closing the active shift | Cashier Supervisor | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 3 | Cashier counts out the excess large bills (PHP 500 and PHP 1,000 denominations) under dual-witness with Supervisor | Cashier | Cashier Supervisor | 3 min |
| 4 | POS prints a skim receipt showing amount, cashier ID, supervisor ID, and timestamp; both sign the physical receipt | System | Cashier Supervisor | 2 min |
| 5 | Supervisor immediately walks to back-office and deposits the skimmed cash into the Smart Safe (W212); Safe logs the deposit with unique transaction ID | Cashier Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 6 | At EOD (W5F), the system calculates Total Cash = (Current Till + Sum of All Skims) for final Z-report balancing | System | Cashier Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Skim / Cash Drop function (real-time threshold alerting)
- Smart Safe integration (W212 — deposit logging, denomination counting)
- EOD Z-report module (W5F — skim-inclusive cash reconciliation)
- LP Exception Dashboard (flagging stores with unusual skim frequency or amounts)
- Retail Store Backend (audit trail of all skim transactions)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Security risk of holding excessive cash in the till during peak hours (robbery / holdup risk in PH retail)
- Skim recording errors (wrong amount entered, receipt not signed) causing EOD reconciliation panic
- Supervisor unavailable during peak hours — cashier waiting with excess cash creates bottlenecks
- Smart Safe malfunctions forcing manual cash handling and delayed deposits
- LP risk: collusive skimming between cashier and supervisor (mitigated by dual-sign and CCTV)
- Discrepancy between POS skim record and Smart Safe deposit amount (requires investigation per W272)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-alert of "alerts Cashier and Supervisor that the till has exceeded the PHP 50,000 cash…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "counts out the excess large bills" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "prints a skim receipt showing amount, cashier ID, supervisor ID, and timestamp" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: dual-sign and CCTV

### Staffing Implication
2-5 skims per POS terminal per day × 3 terminals per store × 200 stores = 1,200-3,000 skims/day chain-wide. Each skim ~5–7 minutes of Supervisor time. Absorbed by existing Cashier Supervisors as a standard operational duty. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate
5-7 min per skim (17-35 min/day per terminal at 2-5 skims/day).
Breakdown: 2 min POS skim transaction initiation + 3 min dual-witness cash count + 2 min smart safe deposit walk (5–7 min total incl. receipt verification at peak).
At 1,200-3,000 skims/day chain-wide across 200 stores (per the staffing derivation), total Supervisor skim labor is ~100–350 hours/day. EOD reconciliation is automated via smart safe integration.

## W432. Solo Parent Discount Compliance (RA 11861)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents Solo Parent ID at POS |
| **Frequency** | High; ~2–4% of retail transactions |
| **Volume** | ~56,000–112,000 transactions/month chain-wide (2–4% of the 2.8M monthly POS volume) |
| **Owner** | Lead Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Solo Parent Customer, Store Manager |

### Background

The **Expanded Solo Parents Welfare Act (RA 11861)** mandates a 10% discount and 12% VAT exemption on baby's milk, baby food and micronutrient supplements, sanitary diapers, and prescribed medicines/vaccines/medical supplies for the solo parent's young children, for solo parents with annual income below PHP 250,000 (RA 11861 IRR; BIR RR 1-2023). For BuildRight this applies to the enumerated child-care items stocked in-store — not to general hardware goods.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Identification**: Customer presents valid Solo Parent ID (issued by LGU/DSWD) and Solo Parent Booklet | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 2 | **Validation**: Cashier verifies Solo Parent ID validity and that each item is on the RA 11861 eligible-item list (no monthly purchase cap under the IRR) | Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 3 | **POS Selection**: Cashier selects "Solo Parent" discount type; system prompts for ID number and name | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **Item Filtering**: System automatically applies 10% discount and VAT-exemption ONLY to eligible SKUs (mapped in Item Master) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Transaction**: Receipt prints with mandatory Solo Parent disclosure (Name, ID#, Gross Sale, Discount, VAT Exempt Sales) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Manual Log**: Cashier records transaction in the physical Solo Parent Logbook as required by the LGU | Cashier | Lead Cashier | 2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Solo Parent Discount Module (RA 11861 logic)
- Item Master "Solo Parent Eligible" flag (W252)
- BIR-compliant receipt printing with Solo Parent fields
- Monthly tax reconciliation for VAT-exempt sales (W90)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **ID and Item-list Verification**: Checking physical Solo Parent IDs against the eligible-item list is slow and prone to error, especially during peak hours.
- **Audit risk**: Improperly applying the discount to non-eligible hardware items leads to BIR penalties and VAT assessments. mitigated by standard operating procedures and system-enforced validation rules
- **Identity Fraud**: Use of expired or borrowed Solo Parent IDs.

### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-issue of "issued by LGU/DSWD" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "verifies Solo Parent ID validity and that each item is on the RA 11861 eligible-item list" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "discount type" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate
~6 min of cashier effort per Solo Parent transaction (ID identification 1 min + eligible-item validation 2 min + POS discount selection 1 min + manual logbook entry 2 min; item filtering and receipt printing automated); at ~2–4% of the 2.8M monthly POS transactions ≈ 56,000–112,000 transactions/month ≈ 5,600–11,200 cashier hours/month chain-wide.


---
## W445. Display & Demo Infrastructure Maintenance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Scheduled preventive maintenance (PM) or report of non-functional store display (e.g., dead lightbulb, leaking demo faucet) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly (Inspections); Daily (Minor repairs) |
| **Volume** | ~50–100 active "live" demos per store |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Maintenance / Utility, Department Supervisor, Vendor Rep |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily Walkthrough**: Supervisor inspects all "Live Demos" (Lighting, Plumbing, Power Tools) for safety and functionality | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 2 | **Fault Logging**: Non-functional displays logged as a "Maintenance Request" in the Store Operations App | Dept Supervisor | — | 5 min |
| 3 | **Safety Isolation**: If a fault is electrical or involves water (leaks), Maintenance immediately isolates the power/water source | Maintenance | — | 10 min |
| 4 | **Repair/Component Replacement**: Maintenance replaces consumables (bulbs, batteries) or fixes minor hardware issues | Maintenance | — | 30–60 min |
| 5 | **Vendor Coordination**: For complex/branded displays (e.g., custom kitchen modules), Supervisor notifies the Vendor Rep for specialized repair | Dept Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 6 | **Completion & Verification**: Display tested for functionality; "Live Demo" status restored in the system | Dept Supervisor | — | 5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Facility Maintenance module (W47) for display-specific work orders
- Vendor portal for reporting issues with branded display units

### Pain Points / Risks
- Customer safety: Exposed wiring or wet floors from leaking demo units.
- Brand image: Burnt-out bulbs in the lighting gallery make the store look poorly managed.
- Slow vendor response for high-tech or proprietary display repairs.


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-inspect of "inspects all" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "logging" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-issue of "issues" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
Absorbed by existing Maintenance/Utility and Department Supervisors.

### Time Estimate
Daily walkthrough 30 min ≈ 182 hours/store/year; each display fault adds ~60–90 min (fault logging 5 min + safety isolation 10 min + repair/component replacement 30–60 min + vendor coordination 10 min + completion verification 5 min) — at a few faults per store per week the repair stream adds ~0.8–4.3 hours/store/week; chain-wide across 200 stores ≈ 45,000–81,000 hours/year of supervisor and maintenance time on ~50–100 live demos per store.

## W463. Catch-Weight & Cut-to-Length Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer selects a variable-unit item (Lumber, Pipe, Wire, Bulk Nails) |
| **Frequency** | High; ~20–30% of big-box hardware transactions |
| **Volume** | Variable (per meter, per board foot, per kg) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Cashier |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Selection**: Customer identifies material; Sales Associate assists in measuring/cutting | Sales Associate | — | 2-5 min |
| 2 | **Measurement**: Associate uses calibrated tool (tape measure, scale, counter) to determine quantity | Sales Associate | Dept Supervisor | 1 min |
| 3 | **Tag Generation**: Associate enters quantity into the Department Tablet; system generates a "Measurement Slip" or temporary barcode | Sales Associate | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **POS Scan**: Cashier scans the slip at POS; system retrieves the price based on Unit of Measure (UOM) (e.g., PHP 150/meter) | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 5 | **Inventory Update**: System reduces inventory in base UOM (e.g., reduces total roll length by 5 meters) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Reconciliation**: Weekly, Department Supervisor performs a "Remnant Audit" to check for unaccounted waste/off-cuts | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 1 hour/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Dual UOM Support (Sales UOM vs. Inventory UOM)
- POS Integration with Department Measurement Slips
- Waste/Off-cut adjustment module (W92)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement Errors**: Manual measurement at the floor level leads to shrinkage or customer overcharging.
- **Remnant Management**: Short off-cuts (e.g., 0.5m pipe) are hard to sell and often discarded without tracking.

### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "counter" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-capture of "enters quantity into the Department Tablet" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-scan of "scans" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Time Estimate
4.5–7.5 min per occurrence (per-step effort 2-5 min + 1 min + 1 min + 30 sec); recurring steps at their own cadence (1 hour/week) — cadence: High; ~20–30% of big-box hardware transactions; annual effort is event-driven (annualize at realized volume).

## W524. Demo / Display Unit Selling at POS

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about purchasing a demo or display unit from the store floor; or Store Manager decides to sell aged display unit to free floor space |
| **Frequency** | Occasional — ~5–10 display unit sales per store per month |
| **Volume** | ~1,000–2,000 display unit sales/month chain-wide (5–10/store/month per the Frequency) |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (condition assessment); Store Manager (pricing approval and POS processing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Store Manager, Customer, Merchandising Coordinator (replacement order) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer expresses interest in purchasing floor display or demo unit (e.g., power tool on display, faucet fixture, appliance demo unit, tile display panel); or Store Manager identifies aged display unit for sale (display unit on floor > 6 months per Merchandising guidelines) | Customer / Store Manager | — | — |
| 2 | Sales Associate inspects display unit condition: cosmetic condition (scratches, dents, discoloration), completeness (all accessories, manuals, remote, hoses, attachments present), functionality (power on test for power tools/appliances, mechanical test for fixtures); documents condition with photos on tablet | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 15 min |
| 3 | Sales Associate enters condition assessment in system: selects display unit SKU, condition grade (A — like new, minor cosmetic; B — visible wear, fully functional; C — significant wear, minor functional limitation; D — functional but poor cosmetic); system records assessment with photos | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 4 | System calculates suggested selling price based on condition grade and display duration: Condition A — 85% of SRP; Condition B — 70% of SRP; Condition C — 55% of SRP; Condition D — 40% of SRP; configurable depreciation schedule per category in item master (W252) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Store Manager reviews condition assessment and suggested price; can adjust price within ±10% of system-suggested price without further approval; price adjustments > 10% require Regional Manager approval via W5B.4a price override process | Store Manager | Regional Manager (if > 10%) | 5 min |
| 6 | Store Manager informs customer of display unit status, condition, and price; discloses that item is a display/demo unit with visible wear; provides modified warranty terms (standard warranty duration reduced by 50% for display units, or manufacturer warranty if still valid); customer verbally agrees | Store Manager / Customer | — | 5 min |
| 7 | Customer agrees to purchase; Store Manager processes sale at POS: scans display unit barcode (system recognizes display unit flag); selects "Display Unit Sale" transaction type; system applies approved price; system prints customer disclosure on receipt: "DISPLAY UNIT — Sold As-Is with [X]-month warranty"; system sets return policy for this transaction to "No Returns — Display Unit Final Sale" per W532 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 8 | Sales Associate removes display unit from floor; safely disconnects power/water/gas if applicable; packages item or provides carry-out assistance per W531; cleans display area and stages for replacement unit | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 20 min |
| 9 | System automatically creates replenishment request for replacement display/demo unit: if item is current assortment → auto-generates internal transfer request (W22) or purchase suggestion (W2A) to restock display; if item is being discontinued → no replacement; system updates planogram compliance status (W86) to show empty display position | System | Merchandising Coordinator | Automated |
| 10 | Monthly: Merchandising Coordinator reviews display unit sales report — units sold, condition grades, average discount from SRP, replacement order status; identifies categories with high display unit turnover for potential display strategy adjustment | Merchandising Coordinator | VP Merchandising | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Display unit condition assessment with photo capture and condition grading (A/B/C/D) (W524.2–3)
- Automated suggested selling price based on condition grade and configurable depreciation schedule per category (W524.4)
- "Display Unit Sale" transaction type with customer disclosure on receipt and modified warranty/return terms (W524.7)
- Display unit flag on item master linking display inventory to saleable inventory (W524.7)
- Automatic replenishment request for replacement display unit (W524.9)
- Planogram compliance update showing empty display position (W524.9, W86)
- Display unit sales analytics: units sold, condition grades, avg discount, replacement status (W524.10)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — POS transaction), W97 (sample/demo inventory — display unit tracking), W445 (display infrastructure maintenance — display condition feed), W86 (planogram compliance — display position tracking), W22 (transfers — replacement sourcing), W2A (auto-replenishment — replacement PO trigger), W252 (item master — display unit flag and depreciation schedule), W33 (warranty — modified warranty registration), W532 (clearance/final sale — return policy override)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer disputes on condition** — customer claims display unit was represented as better condition than received; photo documentation at assessment mitigates
- **Warranty claim complexity** — display units with modified warranty terms create customer confusion when filing claims; system must clearly track warranty terms per display sale transaction
- **Pricing authority abuse** — Store Manager repeatedly pricing display units at maximum discount (40% SRP for Grade D) for accomplice purchases; LP monitoring through W522 daily review mitigates
- **Replacement delays** — display unit sold but replacement takes weeks to arrive, leaving empty display position that degrades store appearance and planogram compliance
- **Safety liability** — selling display units (especially power tools, electrical fixtures) without thorough safety testing creates product liability risk; condition assessment must include functional safety check; mitigated by CTL-259 (controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1))


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-document of "documents condition with photos on tablet" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-capture of "enters condition assessment in system" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates suggested selling price based on condition grade and display duration" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: must clearly track warranty terms per display sale transaction

### Time Estimate
~55 min per display unit sale (15 min condition assessment + 5 min system entry + 5 min pricing review + 5 min customer disclosure + 5 min POS processing + 20 min removal and cleanup). At 5–10 per store per month, total per store is ~275–550 min/month (~5–9 hours/month). Chain-wide: ~1,000–2,000 hours/month.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — display unit selling is absorbed by Sales Associate (assessment and removal) and Store Manager (pricing and POS).
- Monthly Merchandising Coordinator review: ~1 hour/month absorbed by existing Merchandising FTE.
- Total store-level labor for display unit sales: ~5–9 hours/month per store, spread across ~5–10 transactions.

## W562. Store-Level Loss Prevention Daily Routine

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening (LP daily checklist initiation) |
| **Frequency** | Daily at all 200 stores |
| **Volume** | 200 daily checklists completed |
| **Owner** | LP Officer / Store Manager (stores without dedicated LP Officer) |
| **Participants** | LP Officer, Store Manager, Cashiers (C), Stock Associates (C) |

### Background

W37 covers loss prevention exception reporting and investigation. W248 covers store inventory variance and LP investigation. W466 covers the comprehensive Loss Prevention & Asset Protection (LPAP) program. W538 covers real-time POS exception monitoring and alert response. However, there is no workflow covering the daily operational LP routine — the morning checklist, EAS gate testing, blind-spot monitoring, fitting area checks, high-value product verification, till spot-checks, and end-of-day LP summary.

For a 200-store chain with a shrinkage target of less than 1.5%, consistent daily LP execution is critical. The Philippine retail environment faces unique challenges: organized retail crime (ORC) rings targeting hardware tools and premium items, internal theft by staff (especially in stores with low LP coverage), and vendor fraud during DSD receiving. Without a standardized daily LP checklist, LP activities become ad-hoc — some stores have diligent LP Officers while others (without dedicated LP staff) neglect daily LP tasks entirely.

The rotational LP model (20 Regional LP Officers covering 200 stores, each officer visiting ~10 stores on rotation) means that most stores are without a dedicated LP Officer on any given day. On non-visit days, the Store Manager serves as LP proxy. A standardized daily checklist ensures consistent LP execution regardless of whether an LP Officer is physically present.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **LP Officer opens LP dashboard on tablet at store opening**: LP Officer (or Store Manager on non-visit days) logs into LP module on tablet; dashboard displays: (a) today's checklist items, (b) overnight exception alerts from W538 (voids, no-sales, refund patterns, sweet-hearting indicators), (c) pending investigation items from W248, (d) previous day's checklist completion status; Officer acknowledges dashboard and begins daily routine | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | **EAS gate power-on test and functionality verification**: at each store entrance/exit with EAS (Electronic Article Surveillance) gates: (a) power on EAS system, (b) test each gate with test tag (walk through with tagged item — verify alarm triggers), (c) test deactivation pad at POS (scan tagged item through — verify alarm does NOT trigger), (d) record pass/fail per gate in checklist; if gate fails: log maintenance request via W47, implement manual bag-check procedure for affected entrance until repaired | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **High-value product cage/locked display integrity check**: verify security of high-value merchandise: (a) power tool cage — verify lock engaged, key accounted for, inventory count matches expected (spot-check 5 items against system), (b) locked display cases (premium appliances, high-end power tools, specialty hardware) — verify locks engaged, glass intact, item count correct, (c) key log review — verify all high-value key sign-outs from prior day are returned and logged; discrepancies flagged for immediate investigation per W248 | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Review overnight exception alerts from W538**: review POS exception alerts generated overnight and from previous evening: (a) high-value voids (void > PHP 5,000 after 8 PM), (b) excessive no-sale drawer opens (> 3 per cashier per shift), (c) refund patterns (same cashier processing multiple refunds), (d) sweet-hearting indicators (repeated small-quantity scans of expensive items), (e) employee discount abuse patterns; prioritize alerts for follow-up investigation; flag top 3 alerts for Cashier performance discussion (W522) | LP Officer | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **Check CCTV camera coverage — verify all cameras online**: access CCTV system (W71); verify all cameras showing live feed: (a) entrance/exit cameras, (b) POS lane cameras, (c) backroom/receiving door cameras, (d) high-value product cage camera, (e) parking lot cameras; for any offline cameras: log IT helpdesk ticket per W48 with priority "Security System"; document offline camera locations and estimated blind-spot coverage gap; adjust physical patrol frequency for areas with offline cameras | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Blind-spot walkthrough — areas not covered by CCTV**: walk areas with known CCTV blind spots: (a) restrooms and vestibule areas, (b) fitting/trial areas for workwear/PPE, (c) lumber yard corners and behind rack structures, (d) garden center rear sections, (e) receiving dock when not in active DSD receiving; look for: concealed merchandise, discarded packaging (evidence of in-store consumption or tag-switching), unauthorized access points; document observations in checklist | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 7 | **Fitting area / trial room check**: verify fitting area procedures: (a) item count sign-in/sign-out board compliance — are associates counting items in and out?, (b) check trial rooms for concealed merchandise or discarded security tags, (c) verify EAS-detag only happens at POS (not in fitting area), (d) assess whether fitting area associate is actively monitoring; document compliance level | LP Officer | — | 5 min |
| 8 | **DSD receiving observation — spot-check vendor deliveries**: if DSD receiving is occurring during visit: observe 1–2 deliveries per W18 for: (a) vendor identification verification, (b) item count vs. delivery receipt match, (c) quality inspection compliance, (d) short-shipment indicators (seal intact but count low, substitution without authorization), (e) vendor access restricted to receiving area only; if irregularities observed: escalate per W248 investigation protocol | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min (if DSD occurring) |
| 9 | **Mid-day till spot-check on 1 cashier**: select 1 cashier for unannounced till spot-check: (a) pause cashier's current transaction, (b) perform cash count of drawer, (c) compare physical cash to system expected amount per POS cash tracking, (d) variance > PHP 200 (over or short): document per W272 cashier over/short protocol, (e) variance within tolerance: record as "Pass" in checklist; rotate cashier selection daily to cover all cashiers within 2-week cycle | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 10 | **Afternoon high-value shelf count spot-check**: select 5–10 high-value SKUs from tools/hardware department: (a) physically count shelf and backroom quantity, (b) compare to system perpetual inventory (INV-001), (c) variance > 2 units: flag for W248 inventory variance investigation, (d) look for evidence of tag-switching (wrong barcode on high-value item), (e) verify EAS tags present on all items requiring tagging; SKU selection varied daily to cover top 100 high-value SKUs within monthly cycle | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 11 | **Review SCO exception log**: access SCO (self-checkout) exception log per W281: (a) review today's SCO weight-verification exceptions, (b) review SCO attendant interventions, (c) identify patterns (same customer repeatedly triggering weight exceptions, scan-and-pass behavior), (d) review SCO void rate compared to staffed lanes; flag anomalies for investigation | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 12 | **End-of-day LP daily log submission**: LP Officer completes daily checklist in LP module: (a) all checklist items marked pass/fail/NA with notes, (b) summary of findings (exceptions found, investigations opened, maintenance requests), (c) escalation items for follow-up (Regional LP Officer, Store Manager, HR), (d) overall LP risk assessment for the day (Green/Yellow/Red); submit log; system stores daily log and feeds data into: (a) W37 monthly LP dashboard, (b) W466 quarterly LPAP review, (c) W67 store performance scorecard LP metrics | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-050 (real-time LP exception monitoring — overnight alert source)
- POS-068 (new requirement for store LP daily routine — checklist module, dashboard, daily log)
- W37 (LP exception reporting — escalation for investigation)
- W248 (inventory variance investigation — trigger from spot-checks)
- W466 (LPAP program — framework for LP activities and reporting)
- W538 (POS real-time LP monitoring — alert source for overnight exceptions)
- W71 (CCTV audit — camera status verification)
- W272 (cashier over/short — till spot-check protocol)
- W281 (SCO exception — exception log review)
- W18 (DSD receiving — vendor fraud observation)
- W47 (facility maintenance — EAS gate repair, CCTV repair)
- W48 (IT helpdesk — security system tickets)
- W522 (cashier performance — exception alert follow-up)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **200 stores cannot all have dedicated LP Officers** — only ~20 Regional LP Officers for the chain, meaning each store gets a dedicated visit only once every 2 weeks; mitigated by Store Manager serving as LP proxy with abbreviated daily checklist on non-visit days, and automated W538 exception monitoring filling the gap
- **High-value items in outdoor lumber yard are inherently difficult to secure** — lumber, steel bars, and cement in open yard areas cannot be EAS-tagged or caged; mitigated by yard gate control (W438), customer receipt verification at exit, and CCTV coverage with regular inventory counts
- **EAS tagging compliance for small hardware items is impractical** — screws, bolts, drill bits, and small accessories are too small or too low-value for EAS tags; mitigated by peg hook count reconciliation and gravity bin weight verification
- **ORC rings are sophisticated and target multiple stores in sequence** — organized retail crime groups may hit 3–5 stores in a region within a week, exploiting patterns (e.g., visiting during shift change, distracting staff with questions while accomplices steal); mitigated by regional ORC alert network (W466) and inter-store communication via W571 daily communication


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "logs into LP module on tablet" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scan tagged item through — verify alarm does NOT trigger" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "review — verify all high-value key sign-outs from prior day are returned and…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Store Manager serving as LP proxy with abbreviated daily checklist on non-visit days, and automated W538 exception monitoring filling the gap; operational: yard gate control (W438), customer receipt verification at exit, and CCTV coverage with regular inventory counts; operational: peg hook count reconciliation and gravity bin weight verification

### Time Estimate
- Per LP Officer per store per visit day: 1.5–2.5 hours (dashboard 5 min + EAS test 10 min + high-value check 15 min + exception review 15 min + CCTV check 10 min + blind-spot walk 15 min + fitting area 5 min + DSD observation 15 min + till spot-check 10 min + shelf count 15 min + SCO review 10 min + daily log 10 min = ~2 hours 15 min; adjusted for non-visit days with abbreviated checklist: ~1.5 hours)
- **Total chain-wide daily**: ~320 person-hours daily (50 visit-day + 270 non-visit-day, per the decomposition below)
- **Regional LP Officer visit days**: 20 officers × 1 store per day × 2.5 hours = 50 person-hours
- **Non-visit days (Store Manager as proxy)**: 180 stores × 1.5 hours = 270 person-hours (abbreviated checklist)

### Staffing Implication
- 20 Regional LP Officers rotate across stores (~10 stores each); existing headcount sufficient with rotational coverage model
- Store Managers serve as LP proxy on non-visit days; LP daily checklist absorbed into existing Store Manager morning routine (~1.5 hours on non-visit days)
- No incremental headcount needed; LP coverage gap mitigated by W538 automated POS exception monitoring

## W571. Store-Level Daily Communication & Memo Acknowledgment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | HQ issues daily communication, policy update, task assignment, or urgent memo via ERP communication module |
| **Frequency** | ~10–20 communications per day from HQ to stores; Store Manager daily review |
| **Volume** | Higher during promotional changeover (bi-monthly), policy changes, seasonal transitions, price freeze events |
| **Owner** | VP Store Operations (HQ-side); Store Manager (store-side) |
| **Participants** | Store Operations Coordinator, Store Manager, Department Supervisors, IT (system support) |

### Background

No existing workflow covers the HQ-to-store daily communication cascade. For a 200-store chain spanning Mindanao, Visayas, and Luzon, consistent communication execution is critical for: promotional price changes (W40), new policy compliance (W186), recall notices (W29), operational directives, safety alerts, seasonal preparation instructions, HR announcements, and IT maintenance windows.

The communication challenge is amplified by geographic dispersion — stores in different regions may have different LGU regulations, typhoon exposure levels, and market conditions. A typhoon warning for Eastern Visayas requires urgent communication to 15 affected stores but is irrelevant to Mindanao locations. A DTI price freeze on construction materials in a calamity-declared province (W468) requires immediate action by stores in that province only. Without targeted communication, stores receive irrelevant messages, leading to communication fatigue and desensitization to genuinely urgent directives.

Without a structured communication workflow, messages get lost, acknowledged without reading, or executed inconsistently across the chain. Store Managers in a 200-store network process 10–20 HQ communications per day — without a tracking system, there is no visibility into which stores have read and acted on critical communications.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **HQ department creates communication item in ERP**: authorized HQ department (Merchandising, HR, IT, Compliance, Store Operations, Finance, Safety) creates communication item in ERP communication module: (a) subject line (clear, action-oriented), (b) body content (markdown supported with images, tables, file attachments), (c) priority level (Urgent — red, Standard — yellow, Informational — green), (d) target audience (all stores, regional [select region], specific stores [multi-select], by store class [e.g., stores with garden center]), (e) category (promotional, pricing, policy, recall, safety, HR, IT, operations, seasonal), (f) acknowledgment deadline (Urgent: 4 hours, Standard: 24 hours, Informational: 72 hours), (g) execution confirmation required (yes/no — if yes, Store Manager must confirm task completion with optional photo evidence); submit for distribution | HQ Department Lead | VP Store Operations | 10–20 min per communication |
| 2 | **Communication module pushes to targeted Store Managers**: system distributes communication to targeted Store Managers: (a) item appears on Store Manager's ERP dashboard under "Communications" tab, (b) unread count badge displayed on dashboard, (c) communication categorized and color-coded by priority, (d) full content and attachments accessible via dashboard and mobile app | System | — | Automated (within 60 seconds of creation) |
| 3 | **Urgent items trigger push notification and SMS**: for Urgent priority communications: (a) push notification sent to Store Manager's mobile app immediately, (b) SMS sent to Store Manager's registered mobile number with subject line and link (for managers who may not have app open), (c) reminder push notification every 2 hours until acknowledged, (d) if not acknowledged within 4 hours: escalate to Regional Manager (Step 11); for Standard items: appear in daily communication digest email sent at 7:00 AM; for Informational items: appear in weekly digest | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Store Manager opens communication during morning briefing**: Store Manager reviews communications during morning huddle (W5A store opening): (a) opens dashboard on tablet or office PC, (b) reviews new communications sorted by priority (Urgent first), (c) reads full content of each communication, (d) identifies action items requiring cascade to Department Supervisors | Store Manager | — | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **For task-type communications — assign to Department Supervisor**: if communication requires execution (e.g., "Set up promotional endcap for Power Tool Festival by Friday"): (a) Store Manager assigns task to specific Department Supervisor via communication module, (b) sets due date and expected completion evidence (photo, checklist, system confirmation), (c) task appears on Department Supervisor's handheld dashboard, (d) Store Manager tracks task status from communication detail screen | Store Manager | — | 3–5 min per task-type communication |
| 6 | **For policy-type communications — review with supervisors during daily huddle**: if communication is a policy update or operational directive: (a) Store Manager reviews key points with Department Supervisors during morning huddle, (b) supervisors acknowledge understanding verbally and sign huddle attendance log, (c) Store Manager records huddle completion in communication module as documentation of cascade | Store Manager | — | 5–10 min per policy communication |
| 7 | **Store Manager acknowledges receipt and confirms understanding**: Store Manager clicks "Acknowledge" button on each communication: (a) system records timestamp, Store Manager ID, and store ID, (b) acknowledgment status changes to "Read & Acknowledged" with timestamp, (c) communication moves from "Unread" to "Acknowledged" queue but remains accessible; checkbox with text "I have read and understood this communication" — prevents accidental acknowledgment without reading | Store Manager | — | 30 sec per communication |
| 8 | **For execution-required items — confirm completion with evidence**: for communications marked as "execution confirmation required": (a) after Store Manager or Department Supervisor completes the task (e.g., promotional display set up per W523, recall items pulled from shelf per W29), (b) Store Manager opens the communication and clicks "Confirm Completion", (c) uploads required evidence: photo of completed display, screenshot of system confirmation, or checklist sign-off, (d) system records completion timestamp and evidence; Regional Manager can review completion evidence for quality spot-check | Store Manager / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5–10 min per execution confirmation |
| 9 | **System tracks acknowledgment rate and completion status per store**: system maintains real-time tracking dashboard: (a) per communication: acknowledgment rate (X of 200 stores acknowledged), completion rate (for execution-required items), average time-to-acknowledgment, stores not yet acknowledged, (b) per store: acknowledgment compliance rate (last 30 days), overdue items count, average response time, (c) per priority level: Urgent SLA compliance (4-hour target); dashboard accessible to VP Store Operations and Regional Managers | System | — | Automated (real-time tracking) |
| 10 | **Regional Manager reviews non-acknowledging stores and follows up**: Regional Manager reviews communication compliance dashboard: (a) identifies stores with overdue acknowledgments, (b) calls or messages Store Manager directly for follow-up, (c) documents reason for delayed acknowledgment (system issue, Store Manager absence, connectivity problem), (d) if systemic issue (e.g., ISP outage in region): escalates to IT (W48) for resolution and extends acknowledgment deadline for affected stores | Regional Manager | — | 15–30 min/day |
| 11 | **Unacknowledged urgent items after 4 hours escalate to Regional Manager**: for Urgent communications not acknowledged within 4-hour SLA: (a) system auto-escalates to Regional Manager with store name and communication subject, (b) Regional Manager contacts Store Manager via phone call (mandatory — not just message), (c) Regional Manager can acknowledge on behalf of Store Manager with note explaining circumstance (e.g., "Store Manager on emergency leave — I confirmed with Assistant Manager over phone"), (d) escalation logged in audit trail | System / Regional Manager | Regional Manager | 5–10 min per escalation |
| 12 | **Monthly communication compliance dashboard**: system generates monthly compliance report: (a) overall chain-wide acknowledgment rate (target: > 95% within SLA), (b) completion rate for execution-required items, (c) average response time by priority level, (d) bottom 10 stores by compliance rate (flagged for Store Manager coaching), (e) top 5 most impactful communications (highest execution-required volume), (f) communication volume trend (per department, per category); report feeds into W67 store performance review as "Communication Compliance" KPI | System | VP Store Operations | Automated (monthly report generation) |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-069 (new requirement for store communication management — communication module, dashboard, acknowledgment tracking, escalation engine)
- W67 (store performance review — communication compliance KPI)
- W186 (SOP governance — policy update distribution)
- W523 (promotional terminal setup — promo setup task communication)
- W40 (price changes — price update communications)
- W29 (product recall — recall notification distribution)
- W468 (DTI price freeze — targeted regional communication)
- W48 (IT helpdesk — connectivity issue resolution for communication delivery)
- W5A (store opening — morning huddle communication review)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Communication fatigue from excessive HQ messages** — Store Managers receiving 10–20 messages daily may skim without reading, especially Informational items; mitigated by strict categorization (only genuinely urgent items marked Urgent), mandatory reading confirmation checkbox, and HQ communication guidelines limiting daily volume
- **Store Managers may acknowledge without cascading to supervisors** — clicking "Acknowledge" is easy but actually briefing Department Supervisors during huddle is harder; mitigated by periodic spot-checks by Regional Manager and supervisor cascade documentation requirement for policy and task-type communications
- **Language barriers for detailed policy communications** — communications written in English may not be fully understood by all Store Managers (especially in Visayas and Mindanao where primary languages are Cebuano/Bisaya); mitigated by bilingual communication templates (English + Filipino) and regional translation support from Regional Manager office
- **Urgent communications during offline POS events may be delayed** — if store is in offline mode (W535) due to connectivity loss, push notifications and dashboard updates are delayed; mitigated by SMS fallback for Urgent items (SMS does not require ERP connectivity) and regional IT priority for store connectivity restoration


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "HQ department creates communication item in ERP" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "count badge displayed on dashboard" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-escalate of "escalate to Regional Manager" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: strict categorization (only genuinely urgent items marked Urgent), mandatory reading confirmation checkbox, and HQ communication guidelines limiting daily volume; operational: reading confirmation checkbox, and HQ communication guidelines limiting daily volume; operational: periodic spot-checks by Regional Manager and supervisor cascade documentation requirement for policy and task-type communications

### Time Estimate
- Store Manager daily review and acknowledgment: 10–40 min (10–20 communications × 1–2 min each)
- Task assignment to Department Supervisors: 3–5 min per task-type communication (~2–3 per day)
- Execution confirmation: 5–10 min per item (~1–2 per day)
- **Total per store per day**: ~20–40 min for Store Manager; ~10–15 min for Department Supervisors
- **Total chain-wide daily**: 200 stores × 20 min Store Manager + 200 stores × 12 min Supervisors = ~107 person-hours daily
- **Regional Manager follow-up**: 15–30 min/day
- **Monthly compliance reporting**: automated (no incremental time)

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Store Manager and Store Operations team; no incremental headcount needed
- Store Operations Coordinator (HQ-side): communication creation and distribution absorbed into existing role (~1–2 hours/day for 10–20 communications)
- Regional Managers: compliance follow-up absorbed into existing store visit cadence

## W574. Store-Level Daily Closing Procedure

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours end (typically 8:00 PM, or 9:00 PM during extended hours) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 365 days = 73,000 closings/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (or Assistant Manager) |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Lead Cashier, Security Guard, Stock Associate |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Announce last call 15 minutes before closing; PA system announcement in English and Filipino | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 2 | Escort remaining customers to checkout and then to exit; no new transactions after posted closing time | Sales Associate | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | Run Z-report (end-of-day reading) on all POS terminals; verify all terminals report successfully | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 4 | Count cash per drawer against system Z-report totals; flag any variances exceeding PHP 100 per drawer | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 5 | Resolve cashier over/short variances per W272; document unresolved variances for next-day follow-up | Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 6 | Prepare bank deposit per W541 (cash, coin, checks separated; deposit slip completed; smart safe or deposit bag sealed) | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Store security walkthrough: yard/lumber area, receiving dock, paint room, garden center, sales floor aisles, break room, restrooms — check for: remaining customers, safety hazards, open doors/windows, water leaks | Store Manager | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 8 | Verify all equipment secured: paint mixer covered and powered off, lumber/panel saw blade guards on and power disconnected, forklifts parked and keys removed to lockbox, pallet jacks returned to designated area, ladders secured | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 9 | Verify all refrigerated/temperature-sensitive items (adhesives, certain chemicals) are within acceptable range per W587 temperature protocol if applicable | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 10 | Set alarm system; verify all entry points locked (front doors, receiving dock, emergency exits, yard gate, office) | Security Guard | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 11 | Submit daily sales summary to Regional Manager via system dashboard: total sales vs. target, transaction count, top 5 SKUs, cash over/short total, any incidents noted | Store Manager | — | 5 min |

**Total time per closing**: ~75–90 minutes per store per day

### System Touchpoints (W574 — Store-Level Daily Closing Procedure)
- POS Z-report generation per terminal (POS-009)
- Cash drawer count reconciliation vs. system (POS-008, POS-009)
- W272 (cashier over/short dispute resolution for unresolved variances)
- W541 (cash office operations & bank deposit preparation)
- W71 (physical security & access control — alarm system, lock verification)
- W579 (equipment safety check — next-morning counterpart)
- Daily sales summary dashboard for Regional Manager
- BIR daily sales report retention per NFR-006 (10-year retention)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cash count discrepancies delay closing** — if a drawer is significantly over/short (> PHP 500), the Store Manager must investigate before allowing the cashier to leave, extending closing time by 15–30 min; mitigated by mid-day cash sweeps per W278 reducing drawer cash levels
- **Customers refusing to leave at closing** — some customers, especially trade professionals, may resist leaving; mitigated by last-call announcement 15 min before close and polite escort by Sales Associate
- **Overnight security risk if closing procedure rushed** — skipping the walkthrough to leave earlier may miss unlocked doors or safety hazards; mitigated by Store Manager sign-off requirement on digital closing checklist
- **Equipment not properly secured creates DOLE liability** — unsecured saws or forklift keys accessible to unauthorized persons; mitigated by lockbox protocol and morning verification per W579


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "checkout and then to exit" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-report of "report" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-count of "Count cash per drawer against system Z-report totals" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: mid-day cash sweeps per W278 reducing drawer cash levels; operational: last-call announcement 15 min before close and polite escort by Sales Associate; operational: Store Manager sign-off requirement on digital closing checklist

### Staffing Implication
- 1 Store Manager + 1 Lead Cashier + 1 Security Guard per closing; absorbed into existing shift duties
- ~73,000 store closings/year across 200 stores = ~100,000 staff-hours/year for closing procedures
- No incremental headcount needed

### Time Estimate
~80–88 min per store closing (last-call announcements 2 min + customer escort 5–10 min + Z-reports 10 min + cash count 15 min + variance resolution 5–10 min + deposit preparation 10 min + security walkthrough 15 min + equipment verification 5 min + refrigeration check 3 min + alarm set 5 min + sales summary 5 min); × 200 stores × 365 days ≈ 73,000 closings/year ≈ 97,000–107,000 hours/year chain-wide (~480–532 hours/store/year, absorbed by closing staff).


---

## W575. Store-Level Weekly Sales & Operations Review

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Every Monday morning (or first working day of the week) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly per store; Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 52 weeks = 10,400 reviews/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, Regional Manager (for flagged stores) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Store Manager reviews system-generated weekly scorecard: sales vs. budget by department, average transaction value (ATV), transaction count trend, gross margin by department | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 2 | Review promotional sell-through vs. plan for any promotions active during the week | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 3 | Review customer satisfaction scores and top 3 complaint categories per W597 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 4 | Review shrinkage and LP incidents; compare vs. prior week and vs. regional average | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 5 | Review staffing effectiveness: labor cost % of revenue, overtime hours, absenteeism rate per W594 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 6 | Review facility and equipment issues: open maintenance work orders per W47, equipment safety check failures per W579 | Store Manager | — | 3 min |
| 7 | Identify top 3 action items for the coming week with owners, deadlines, and expected outcomes | Store Manager | Department Supervisors | 10 min |
| 8 | Brief Department Supervisors on key findings and action items during Monday morning huddle | Store Manager | Department Supervisors | 10 min |
| 9 | Submit weekly report to Regional Manager via system (auto-populated from scorecard with Store Manager commentary) | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 10 | Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores by sales performance and takes action (call, visit, or escalation to VP Store Operations) | Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 30 min/week across portfolio |

**Total time**: ~55–60 min for Store Manager; ~30 min/week for Regional Manager across portfolio

### System Touchpoints (W575 — Weekly Sales & Operations Review)
- System-generated weekly scorecard (sales, ATV, transactions, margin by department)
- W67 (monthly store performance review — monthly deep-dive complement)
- W522 (daily transaction review — daily data feeding weekly scorecard)
- W35 (management reporting rhythm — consolidated reporting)
- W597 (complaint escalation — CSAT and complaint data feed)
- W594 (attendance verification — absenteeism and overtime data)
- W562 (LP daily routine — shrinkage data)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Scorecard review becomes perfunctory** — Store Managers may treat the weekly review as a checkbox exercise; mitigated by Regional Manager follow-up on bottom-quartile stores and requiring Store Manager commentary on variance explanations
- **Action items not tracked to completion** — weekly action items created but not followed through; mitigated by carrying forward incomplete items to next week's review and tracking in system
- **Regional Manager bandwidth across ~33 stores** (per VS-177) — reviewing all stores weekly is not feasible; mitigated by focusing on bottom quartile and exception-flagged stores only


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews system-generated weekly scorecard" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check failures per W579" (replaces manual Step 6).
- System auto-identify of "Identify top 3 action items for the coming week with owners, deadlines, and…" (replaces manual Step 7).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Regional Manager follow-up on bottom-quartile stores and requiring Store Manager commentary on variance explanations; operational: carrying forward incomplete items to next week's review and tracking in system; operational: focusing on bottom quartile and exception-flagged stores only

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Store Manager and Regional Manager roles; no incremental headcount
- ~200 stores × ~58 min/week ≈ ~10,000 person-hours/year for Store Manager weekly reviews
- Regional Manager weekly review absorbed into existing management cadence

### Time Estimate
~58 min per store weekly review (scorecard 10 min + promotional sell-through 5 min + satisfaction scores 5 min + shrink/LP incidents 5 min + staffing effectiveness 5 min + facility issues 3 min + top-3 action items 10 min + supervisor briefing 10 min + weekly report 5 min); × 200 stores × 52 weeks ≈ 10,400 reviews/year ≈ 10,000 hours/year of Store Manager time; Regional Managers add 30 min/week of bottom-quartile portfolio review.

## W578. Store-Level Payday Weekend & Peak Day Operational Readiness

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Predictable high-traffic days: paydays (15th, 30th/31st), holidays, sale events, month-end |
| **Frequency** | ~4–6 predictable peak days per month per store |
| **Volume** | Payday weekends show 30–50% sales uplift vs. normal weekend; contractor trade customers spike on 15th/30th |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Lead Cashier, Stock Associates, LP |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **3 days before**: system analyzes historical sales patterns by store for upcoming peak day — predicts transaction volume, ATV, peak hours, and top-selling categories | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 2 | **2 days before**: increase cash floats per forecast (typical payday float: 1.5× normal); coordinate with CIT service per W174 for additional cash delivery | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | **2 days before**: inventory top-up for fast-moving SKUs identified by forecast — coordinate with backroom Stock Associates for shelf stocking per W554 | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 hours |
| 4 | **1 day before**: confirm additional cashier deployment per W34 (flex up to all terminals); activate backup POS terminal if normally idle; verify POS system connectivity | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 5 | **1 day before**: queue management preparation — set up express lane, stage mPOS tablets per W206, activate scan & go per W547 | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 6 | **Day of — morning**: all-hands briefing: expected traffic, key promotions active, express lane assignments, LP heightened awareness | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 7 | **Day of — during peak**: Store Manager monitors queue length and checkout wait times; activates queue-busting (mPOS, additional cashier) if wait exceeds 5 minutes; LP patrols high-theft departments | Store Manager | — | Continuous |
| 8 | **Day after**: post-peak flash report — actual vs. predicted sales, checkout wait times, stockouts, LP incidents | Store Manager | — | 10 min |

**Total time**: ~2.5–3 hours preparation + active monitoring during peak + 10 min post-report

### System Touchpoints (W578 — Payday Weekend & Peak Day Operational Readiness)
- Historical sales pattern analysis engine (POS transaction data)
- W34 (shift scheduling — additional cashier deployment)
- W174 (CIT & armored car — cash float increase)
- W212 (smart safe integration — cash management)
- W206 (mobile POS — queue-busting)
- W547 (scan & go — self-scan checkout)
- W554 (shelf replenishment — inventory top-up)
- W562 (LP daily routine — heightened awareness)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Forecast underestimation** — if actual traffic exceeds prediction, cash shortages and stockouts occur; mitigated by conservative 1.5× cash float rule and maintaining safety stock for top 100 SKUs
- **All terminals needed simultaneously** — all 3 terminals active during peak; if one terminal is down per W265, checkout capacity is reduced by 33% during highest-traffic period; mitigated by pre-peak terminal verification (Step 4) and spare terminal pre-positioning during Ber months
- **Contractor rush at opening** — trade professionals arrive at store opening on paydays to buy materials before heading to job sites; this creates an early-morning surge that can overwhelm standard opening staffing; mitigated by opening with full cashier deployment on payday mornings (all 3 terminals from 8:00 AM)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-analyze of "analyzes historical sales patterns by store for upcoming peak day — predicts…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-forecast of "2 days before" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System workflow orchestration of "coordinate with backroom Stock Associates for shelf stocking per W554" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: conservative 1; operational: pre-peak terminal verification (Step 4) and spare terminal pre-positioning during Ber months; operational: opening with full cashier deployment on payday mornings (all 3 terminals from 8:00 AM)

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing store staff; peak-day flex is absorbed by shift overlap scheduling per W34
- ~200 stores × ~3 hours preparation × 6 peak days/month = ~3,600 staff-hours/month
- No incremental headcount needed

### Time Estimate
~3.1 hours per peak-day preparation (cash float increase 15 min + inventory top-up coordination 2 hours + cashier deployment confirmation 15 min + queue setup 15 min + morning briefing 10 min + post-peak flash report 10 min) plus continuous queue monitoring during the peak; at ~4–6 peak days/store/month ≈ 12.4–18.6 hours/store/month ≈ 30,000–45,000 hours/year chain-wide across 200 stores (forecast analysis automated).

## W581. Store-Level Vendor Representative Access & Activity Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Vendor representative (promodizer, merchandiser, product trainer, brand ambassador) arrives at store |
| **Frequency** | ~5–15 vendor reps per store per day; ~1,000–3,000 visits chain-wide daily |
| **Volume** | ~500–1,500 active vendor representatives across 200 stores (supporting ~1,000–3,000 chain-wide visits/day per the Frequency) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisors, Security Guard, Vendor Representative |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Vendor rep arrives at store entrance; presents government-issued ID to Security Guard; Guard verifies vendor rep is on approved vendor representative list (maintained by Store Manager and updated monthly per vendor coordination) | Security Guard | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 2 | Security Guard issues temporary visitor badge with: vendor name, company, date, time-in; badge is time-limited (max 8 hours); vendor rep signs visitor log | Security Guard | — | 1 min |
| 3 | Vendor rep directed to relevant Department Supervisor for activity assignment; Supervisor reviews planned activities (vendor visit schedule provided by Buyer/Category Manager in advance via system) | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 4 | Department Supervisor assigns specific tasks: (a) shelf stocking/replenishment for vendor products, (b) product demonstration for customers, (c) inventory audit of vendor SKUs, (d) product knowledge training for store staff, (e) promotional display setup per W262, (f) price tag update per W63 | Department Supervisor | — | 2 min |
| 5 | Vendor rep executes assigned activities under Department Supervisor oversight; time tracked via badge scan (check-in/check-out at department zones) | Vendor Representative | Department Supervisor | 2–6 hours |
| 6 | Activity output verification: Department Supervisor reviews completed work — shelf stocking completed, demo materials cleaned up, inventory counts reconciled, training attendance documented | Department Supervisor | — | 5–10 min |
| 7 | Incident reporting: if vendor rep causes any issue (customer complaint, safety violation, merchandise damage, unauthorized area access), Department Supervisor documents incident and reports to Store Manager | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min per incident |
| 8 | Vendor rep checks out at security desk: badge returned, time-out recorded; Security Guard collects badge and signs out visitor log | Security Guard | — | 1 min |
| 9 | Weekly: Store Manager compiles vendor rep activity summary (visits, hours, activities completed, incidents); shared with Buyer/Category Manager for vendor coordination per W269 | Store Manager | — | 15 min/week |

**Total time per visit**: ~5 min store overhead + 2–6 hours vendor activity + 5–10 min verification

### System Touchpoints (W581 — Vendor Representative Access & Activity Management)
- Visitor management system (badge issuance, time tracking)
- W71 (store physical security — access control and visitor management)
- W269 (vendor promodizer management — vendor coordination)
- W449 (promodizer labor compliance — DOLE D.O. 174 compliance)
- W63 (shelf label & price tag — vendor-assisted price updates)
- W262 (store promotional setup — vendor-assisted display build)
- Vendor representative master list maintained in vendor master per W287

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Unscheduled vendor visits disrupt operations** — vendor reps arriving without prior coordination may not have planned activities and take Department Supervisor time away from customer service; mitigated by advance scheduling requirement (vendor must coordinate visit 24 hours in advance via Buyer)
- **Vendor reps accessing unauthorized areas** — vendor reps may wander into stockroom, office, or receiving areas beyond their authorized zone; mitigated by zone-specific badge access and Security Guard monitoring
- **Vendor reps giving incorrect product information** — vendor reps may provide biased or inaccurate product information favoring their brand; mitigated by requiring Department Supervisor presence during customer demos and BuildRight product knowledge training materials as authoritative source
- **Labor compliance risk per DOLE D.O. 174** — vendor promodizers working in stores may be deemed BuildRight employees under Philippine labor law if not properly documented; mitigated by W449 compliance protocol (vendor-employed, vendor-paid, clear contractor status documentation)


### Automation Opportunity
- System rule-based auto-approval of "approved vendor representative list" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "Security Guard issues temporary visitor badge with" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "reviews planned activities" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: advance scheduling requirement (vendor must coordinate visit 24 hours in advance via Buyer); operational: zone-specific badge access and Security Guard monitoring; operational: requiring Department Supervisor presence during customer demos and BuildRight product knowledge training materials as authoritative source

### Staffing Implication
- Security Guard visitor processing: ~2 min × 10 visits/day = ~20 min/day (absorbed)
- Department Supervisor oversight: ~10 min per visit × 10 visits/day = ~100 min/day (absorbed)
- Store Manager weekly summary: ~15 min/week (absorbed)
- No incremental headcount needed

### Time Estimate
~14–19 min of store-staff time per vendor-rep visit (security check-in/out 3 min + supervisor direction 3 min + task assignment 2 min + output verification 5–10 min; incident reporting 5 min per incident as they arise) — the rep's own 2–6 hours on the floor are vendor-side; at ~1,000–3,000 visits/day chain-wide ≈ 233–950 staff-hours/day ≈ 85,000–347,000 hours/year absorbed within Department Supervisor floor duties, plus the weekly activity summary 15 min/week ≈ 13 hours/store/year.
