## W606. Store-Level Water & Utility Conservation Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of billing cycle; daily monitoring |
| **Frequency** | Daily monitoring; monthly review per utility bill cycle |
| **Volume** | 200 stores; ~PHP 650,000–1,100,000/month utility cost per store (consistent with W111's PHP 80–150/sqm/month over 8,000–15,000 sqm; electricity, water, gas for forklift) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, Maintenance Staff, Finance Analyst, Facilities Coordinator |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-captures utility meter readings via IoT sensors (electricity, water) where installed; manual meter readings captured by Maintenance Staff daily at stores without IoT | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | System calculates daily utility consumption vs. budget allocation; displays consumption per sqm benchmark on Store Manager dashboard; alerts on > 15% daily variance from 30-day average | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Store Manager conducts daily conservation walkthrough: (a) verify air conditioning set to 24°C policy, (b) check for water leaks in garden center and restrooms, (c) verify non-selling area lights off during daytime, (d) check forklift charging schedule (off-peak hours), (e) verify lumber yard sprinkler timer operation | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 4 | If leak or waste detected: Store Manager creates maintenance work order (per W47) with urgency classification; for water emergencies (burst pipe), activates immediate shutoff and emergency repair protocol | Store Manager | Facilities Coordinator | 5 min |
| 5 | Monthly: Finance Analyst receives utility bills per store; system auto-compares bill to meter reading data; flags discrepancies > 10% for investigation (billing error, meter malfunction, theft) | Finance Analyst | Controller | 30 min/store |
| 6 | Monthly: Store Manager reviews utility cost as % of store operating budget (W489); identifies top 3 utility cost drivers; implements conservation action items for next month | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 20 min |
| 7 | Quarterly: Facilities Coordinator conducts utility benchmark analysis across 200 stores — identifies bottom 10% performers (highest utility cost per sqm); coordinates energy audit for worst-performing stores; shares best practices from top 10% performers | Facilities Coordinator | COO | 4 hours |
| 8 | Annually: Facilities Coordinator evaluates utility cost reduction projects (LED retrofit, solar panel installation per W173, water recycling for garden centers, inverter AC upgrade); presents business case to CFO for capital approval (per W21) | Facilities Coordinator | CFO | 8 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Facilities module: meter reading capture, utility consumption tracking, benchmark analytics
- ERP Finance module: utility bill processing, cost allocation to store P&L (W489)
- IoT platform: real-time utility monitoring and alerting (where installed)
- BI dashboard: store-level utility cost trends, benchmarking, conservation tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **IoT sensor gaps**: not all 200 stores have IoT metering; manual readings are inconsistent and may be skipped; mitigated by daily reading checklist with compliance tracking
- **Utility billing errors**: Philippine utility providers (MERALCO, Davao Light, VECO) occasionally issue incorrect bills; mitigated by automated bill-to-meter comparison in Step 5
- **Tenant-shared utilities**: some stores share utility infrastructure with other tenants in commercial complexes; mitigated by lease-level utility allocation agreements tracked in W117


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "captures utility meter readings via IoT sensors" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates daily utility consumption vs." (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "check for water leaks in garden center and restrooms" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: daily reading checklist with compliance tracking; operational: automated bill-to-meter comparison in Step 5; operational: lease-level utility allocation agreements tracked in W117

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~10 min/day on conservation walkthrough + ~20 min/month on utility review = ~5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Maintenance Staff**: ~5 min/day on meter readings = ~2.5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Analyst**: ~30 min/store/month × 200 stores = ~100 hours/month; distributed across FP&A team (3-4 analysts) = ~25 hours each. Absorbed within existing roles.

### Time Estimate
Daily per-store cycle 15–20 min (meter capture 5 min + conservation walkthrough 10 min + waste work-order 5 min as needed) ≈ 91–122 hours/store/year ≈ 18,250–24,100 hours/year chain-wide; monthly bill-versus-meter reconciliation 30 min/store + utility budget review 20 min/store ≈ 2,000 hours/year; quarterly benchmark analysis 4 hours and annual reduction-project evaluation 8 hours — total ≈ 20,300–26,200 hours/year (consumption dashboards automated).

## W665. Store-Level KPI Dashboard & Daily Performance Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily system-generated KPI refresh at 06:00 |
| **Frequency** | Daily dashboard review; weekly Store Manager review; monthly Regional Manager consolidation |
| **Volume** | 200 stores generating daily KPI snapshots |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (daily); Regional Manager (weekly consolidation) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Stock Associate, Cashier Lead, Regional Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-generates overnight KPI snapshot per store from POS, inventory, and workforce data: sales (revenue vs. budget, ATV, transaction count, units per transaction), labor (cost % of revenue, overtime hours, absenteeism rate), inventory (out-of-stock count on top 100 SKUs, shrinkage incidents, receiving backlog), customer (NPS pulse, complaint count, queue wait time from W526), fulfillment (BOPIS SLA compliance, delivery orders pending) | System | BI Analyst | Automated |
| 2 | Store Manager reviews dashboard by 08:00 on tablet; system highlights Red metrics (> 10% below target) requiring immediate action | Store Manager | Store Manager | 15-20 min |
| 3 | Store Manager assigns corrective actions for Red metrics: sales shortfall → promotional push with trade counter, labor cost overrun → adjust next-day schedule per W34, OOS items → expedite replenishment per W596 | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 4 | Department Supervisors review department-level KPIs during morning huddle (sales by department, shelf gaps, customer feedback) | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15-20 min |
| 5 | System generates weekly store scorecard with peer comparison (quartile ranking within region) | System | BI Analyst | Automated |
| 6 | Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores weekly; top-quartile stores flagged for best-practice documentation | Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 3-4 hours |
| 7 | Monthly: system generates 200-store consolidated dashboard for VP Store Operations with trend analysis, outlier flagging, and regional heat map | System | BI Analyst | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- BI dashboard module (daily KPI snapshot, weekly scorecard, monthly consolidated dashboard)
- POS transaction data (sales revenue, ATV, transaction count, units per transaction)
- Inventory module (OOS count, shrinkage incidents, receiving backlog)
- HR attendance data (overtime hours, absenteeism rate)
- Customer feedback module (NPS pulse, complaint count, queue wait time from W526)
- Order management module (BOPIS SLA compliance, delivery orders pending)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Dashboard fatigue from too many metrics — store teams may ignore alerts if over-alerted
- Data latency for real-time operational decisions; overnight batch may not capture late-evening POS adjustments
- Regional comparison unfairness across urban vs. provincial stores with different traffic patterns and competitive landscapes
- Gaming of metrics (e.g., suppressing overtime by understaffing, inflating ATV by splitting transactions)
- Inconsistent department-level data entry across 200 stores affecting KPI accuracy


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "System auto-generates overnight KPI snapshot per store from POS" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "reviews dashboard by 08:00 on tablet" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "counter, labor cost overrun → adjust next-day schedule per W34, OOS items →…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Store Manager (daily); Regional Manager (weekly consolidation) review and approval gate

### Time Estimate
- Store Manager daily: 15-20 min; weekly review: 1 hour
- Regional Manager weekly: 3-4 hours
- Monthly consolidation: automated

### Staffing Implication
- Store Manager: ~20 min/day + 1 hour/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- Regional Manager: ~4 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- No incremental headcount required.

## W667. Store-Level Price Verification & Daily Compliance Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily morning opening routine; system price change alerts |
| **Frequency** | Daily spot-check (morning); weekly comprehensive shelf walk |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~35,000 active SKUs; daily price-change bulletins affecting a handful of SKUs/store (chain-wide ~10–25 changes/day per W40; peaks to ~200 SKUs/store during promo/seasonal transitions) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (daily); Store Manager (weekly audit) |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate, Cashier Lead |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System pushes overnight price changes to POS terminals per W533 and POS-047; Stock Associate receives daily price change notification on handheld listing affected SKUs with old and new prices | System / Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5-10 min |
| 2 | Stock Associate prints and replaces shelf labels/price tags for all changed SKUs per W181; verifies label matches new system price; flags items where shelf label cannot be located (potential out-of-shelf) | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 20-25 min |
| 3 | Department Supervisor performs morning spot-check: scans 20-30 random SKUs across departments with handheld price verification tool; system compares scanned barcode price vs. POS system price vs. shelf label | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 4 | For any mismatches: Stock Associate immediately corrects shelf label and verifies POS price; if POS price is incorrect, Department Supervisor escalates to Store Manager for manual price override per W553 | Stock Associate / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5-10 min |
| 5 | Weekly: Store Manager conducts comprehensive shelf walk in one department (rotating weekly across 4 departments); verifies price accuracy, promotional display pricing per W523, and DTI price freeze compliance per POS-024 | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2 hours |
| 6 | Store Manager logs price verification results in compliance module; system calculates store-level price accuracy rate (target ≥ 99%) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 15-20 min |
| 7 | Monthly: system generates price compliance report for Regional Manager with store ranking; bottom-quartile stores receive corrective action plan | System | Regional Manager | Automated |
| 8 | Price verification data feeds into W69 (price compliance audit) and W553 (pricing error detection) | System | Compliance Team | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS price master (central price repository, promotional pricing)
- Shelf label printing module (label generation, batch printing)
- Handheld price verification tool (barcode scan, price comparison)
- Compliance tracking module (accuracy rate calculation, audit trail)
- DTI price freeze compliance module per POS-024

### Pain Points / Risks
- High volume of price changes during promotional transitions (monthly promo calendar, seasonal events) — up to 200 SKUs/store/day during peak
- Shelf label damage obscuring prices in high-traffic aisles (construction materials, garden center)
- Catch-weight items priced per unit vs. per kg causing customer and staff confusion
- DTI price freeze requiring immediate manual intervention across 200 stores with limited grace period
- Inconsistent handheld scanner battery life affecting morning spot-check completion
- Promotional display pricing mismatches when marketing materials arrive before POS price updates


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "prints and replaces shelf labels/price tags for all changed SKUs per W181" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-scan of "scans 20-30 random SKUs across departments with handheld price verification tool" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-escalate of "escalates to Store Manager for manual price override per W553" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate
- Daily spot-check: 30 min (label replacement + random scan)
- Weekly comprehensive shelf walk: 2 hours
- Monthly reporting: automated

### Staffing Implication
- Stock Associate: ~30 min/day label replacement + 30 min/week comprehensive walk. Absorbed within existing role.
- Department Supervisor: ~15 min/day spot-check. Absorbed within existing role.
- Store Manager: ~2 hours/week shelf walk. Absorbed within existing role.
- No incremental headcount required.

## W702. New Store Opening Project Management & Go-Live Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Board-approved new store location identified; lease signed per W635; construction/renovation nearing completion per W96/W223 |
| **Frequency** | ~10–15 new store openings/year (growth plan per profile §3.1) |
| **Volume** | Each new store opening is a 3-6 month project involving 10+ departments and 150+ individual tasks |
| **Owner** | VP Store Operations (project sponsor); Store Opening Project Manager |
| **Participants** | Real Estate Manager, Construction Manager, CIO, HR Manager, Merchandising Manager, Procurement Manager, Marketing Manager, Finance Controller, Compliance Manager, Supply Chain Manager, Store Manager (appointed) |

### Background

W16 covers the high-level new store opening process. W623 covers the cross-functional readiness review. W223-W227 cover engineering and construction. W96 covers store renovation. However, no workflow provides comprehensive project management for the full new store opening lifecycle — from lease signing through construction, fit-out, staffing, merchandising, system setup, training, soft launch, and grand opening. Each new store represents a PHP 20-50M investment and requires precise coordination across departments to open on time, on budget, and fully operational.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project initiation**: VP Store Operations appoints Store Opening Project Manager: (a) define project scope: location, target opening date, store format (standard, compact, express), estimated budget; (b) establish project governance: steering committee (VP Store Operations, CFO, CIO), weekly status reporting, milestone review gates; (c) create project plan in PPM tool: 150+ tasks across 10 workstreams with dependencies, durations, and owners; (d) major milestones: lease signed, construction start, construction complete, systems installed, inventory delivered, staff hired and trained, soft launch, grand opening | Project Manager | VP Store Operations | 1 week |
| 2 | **Construction & fit-out**: Construction Manager manages build-out per W223: (a) obtain LGU building permits and fire safety permit per W476; (b) construction execution per W224; (c) POS counter, racking, display fixtures, paint mixing station, lumber cutting area installation per W225; (d) final inspection and commissioning per W227; (e) obtain Fire Safety Inspection Certificate (FSIC) from BFP; (f) obtain LGU Certificate of Occupancy; (g) project manager monitors construction milestones and budget | Construction Manager | VP Store Operations | 3-4 months |
| 3 | **Utilities & IT infrastructure**: Facilities Manager and IT coordinate: (a) electricity connection and transformer installation per W701 (3-6 month lead time — initiated during construction); (b) water connection per W701; (c) internet/telecom connection per W701; (d) POS terminals installed and tested (3 terminals per store per model-company profile); (e) network infrastructure: WiFi, CCTV, security alarm system per W71; (f) ERP system configuration: new location created in location master per W508, POS terminal mapping, printer setup, scanner configuration; (g) backoffice computers and handheld devices provisioned | Facilities Manager / IT Infrastructure Lead | CIO | Overlaps with construction |
| 4 | **Regulatory & compliance setup**: Compliance Officer coordinates: (a) BIR branch registration (apply for BIR Certificate of Registration for new branch — TIN, registered address, books of accounts); (b) LGU business permit for new location; (c) BIR-printed or BIR-registered POS invoices for new branch; (d) DTI price board display compliance; (e) fire safety permit per W476; (f) BFP fire extinguisher inspection; (g) DENR permit if applicable (garden center with water discharge); (h) coordinate with W54 regulatory permits workflow | Compliance Officer | VP Legal | 2-3 months |
| 5 | **Staffing & training**: HR Manager coordinates: (a) recruit Store Manager (2-3 months before opening); (b) Store Manager recruits team per the §12.1 roster: 1 ASM, 4 Department Supervisors, 12 Sales Associates, 3 Cashiers, 2 Receiving Clerks, 4 Stock Associates, 1 CSR, 1 Maintenance (~29 FTE per store); (c) all staff complete onboarding per W15 and safety training per W655; (d) cashiers complete POS training per W518; (e) stock associates complete receiving and inventory training per W109; (f) Store Manager and Supervisors complete management training; (g) all certifications current per W655 before opening day | HR Manager / Store Manager | CHRO | 2-3 months before opening |
| 6 | **Merchandising & inventory**: Merchandising Manager and Supply Chain Manager coordinate: (a) planogram design for new store format per W86; (b) initial assortment selection based on store format and local market per W679; (c) initial stock order placed 4-6 weeks before opening per W2C; (d) initial inventory received and putaway per W109 (typically over 2-3 days); (e) shelf stocking and display setup per W573; (f) price tags and shelf labels printed and placed per W667; (g) promotional displays for grand opening per W583 | Merchandising Manager / Supply Chain Manager | VP Merchandising | 4-6 weeks before opening |
| 7 | **Soft launch & readiness verification**: 1-2 weeks before grand opening: (a) conduct soft launch: open store to employees, friends, and family for 2-3 days; (b) test all systems under load: POS transactions, loyalty enrollment, card payments, ecommerce BOPIS routing; (c) verify inventory accuracy: spot-check 100 SKUs across all departments; (d) verify regulatory compliance: BIR invoice printing, senior citizen/PWD discount per W170, price display per W667; (e) Store Manager conducts final walk-through: cleanliness, signage, safety, merchandising standards; (f) fix any issues identified during soft launch | Project Manager / Store Manager | VP Store Operations | 1-2 weeks |
| 8 | **Grand opening**: (a) Marketing coordinates grand opening event per W142: local advertising, social media, PR, community engagement; (b) VP Store Operations or Regional Manager attends grand opening; (c) Store Manager manages opening day operations with full staffing; (d) first-day performance tracked: sales, transactions, customer count, loyalty signups; (e) IT and Helpdesk on standby for system issues per W48 | Marketing / Store Manager | VP Store Operations | 1 day |
| 9 | **Post-opening stabilization**: 30 days after opening: (a) Project Manager conducts post-opening review: sales vs. plan, customer feedback, operational issues, staffing adequacy; (b) Store Manager receives intensive coaching from Regional Manager during first 90 days; (c) merchandising adjustments based on actual sales mix vs. plan; (d) project close-out: final budget reconciliation, lessons learned documentation, project file archived per W255; (e) handover from Project Manager to Regional Manager for ongoing management | Project Manager / Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 30 days post-opening |

### System Touchpoints

- Project portfolio management (PPM) tool for task tracking and milestone management
- ERP location master per W508 (new store setup)
- POS configuration and terminal management per POS-001
- BIR registration module per FIN-010
- HR recruitment and onboarding per W15, W518
- Merchandising and planogram per W86
- Inventory and replenishment per W2C, W109
- Utility connection management per W701
- Regulatory permits per W54
- Marketing campaign per W142
- Capex tracking per W184

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Construction delays**: LGU building permit delays, contractor performance issues, and material supply disruptions (especially in provincial areas) can push opening date by months
- **Cross-departmental coordination failure**: 10+ departments must deliver on parallel timelines; any single department delay (IT installation, regulatory permits, initial inventory) can delay the entire opening
- **New store underperformance**: initial sales may not meet projections; Merchandising must be ready to quickly adjust assortment and pricing based on actual customer demand
- **Staff quality at provincial locations**: recruiting qualified Store Managers and experienced staff for provincial locations is challenging; training may need to be more intensive
- **Utility connection delays**: electricity and internet connections for new locations (especially in provincial areas) can take 3-6 months and are often on the critical path


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "review gates" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-inspect of "inspection and commissioning per W227" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-scan of "Utilities & IT infrastructure" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: VP Store Operations (project sponsor); Store Opening Project Manager review and approval gate; operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication

- **Store Opening Project Manager** (1 FTE or dedicated role per opening): full-time during 3-6 month opening project. Can manage 2-3 concurrent openings.
- **Construction Manager**: dedicated during construction phase per W223.
- **Department resources**: ~10–20% of each department head's time during opening project. Absorbed within existing roles.
- **New store staff**: ~25–30 FTE per new store, hired per W15.

### Time Estimate

- Full project duration: 3-6 months from lease signing to grand opening
- Project initiation: 1 week
- Construction: 3-4 months
- Staffing & training: 2-3 months
- Merchandising setup: 4-6 weeks
- Soft launch: 1-2 weeks
- Post-opening stabilization: 30 days

## W703. Store Closure, Consolidation & Asset Recovery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Board-approved store closure/consolidation decision; lease expiry with non-renewal; sustained underperformance; strategic market exit; force majeure (disaster damage beyond repair) |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc; estimated 2-5 closures/consolidations per year based on portfolio optimization |
| **Volume** | Each closure is a 2-4 month project; store inventory value typically PHP 15-30M per location |
| **Owner** | VP Store Operations (project sponsor) |
| **Participants** | Real Estate Manager, Store Manager, HR Manager, Finance Controller, CIO, Merchandising Manager, Supply Chain Manager, Legal Counsel, Facilities Manager |

### Background

W45 covers the high-level store closure/relocation process. However, a store closure is a complex multi-departmental project requiring careful management of inventory liquidation, asset recovery, employee separation, lease termination, regulatory filings, and customer communication. Mishandled closures result in inventory write-offs, lease penalties, employee lawsuits (illegal dismissal per Philippine labor law), and brand damage in the local community.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Closure decision & announcement**: (a) Board approves closure with specific rationale: underperformance, lease expiry, strategic consolidation, or force majeure; (b) VP Store Operations appoints Closure Project Manager; (c) Legal reviews lease termination provisions: notice period required (typically 60-90 days), early termination penalties, security deposit recovery, restoration obligations; (d) communicate closure timeline to Store Manager (confidential until public announcement); (e) public announcement: VP Store Operations and Marketing coordinate community and media communication; (f) customer notification per W708: notify loyalty members in store's trade area of closure and nearest alternative location | VP Store Operations / Legal | CEO | 1-2 weeks |
| 2 | **Employee separation**: HR Manager coordinates: (a) DOLE-compliant separation process per Philippine Labor Code: 30-day written notice to affected employees, report to DOLE Regional Office per Article 298 (formerly 283) for closures affecting 50+ employees; (b) separation pay computation: 1 month pay per year of service for closures due to redundancy or retrenchment per W643 final pay framework; (c) offer relocation to nearby BuildRight stores for employees willing to transfer (especially Store Managers and Supervisors); (d) outplacement support: job fair with local employers, reference letters, DOLE employment assistance referral; (e) ensure all final pay, 13th month, unused leave, and tax documents (BIR 2316) are prepared per W643 | HR Manager | CHRO | 30-60 days |
| 3 | **Inventory liquidation**: Merchandising Manager coordinates: (a) plan liquidation timeline: 4-6 weeks of clearance sales before final closure; (b) progressive markdown schedule: week 1-2: 20-30% off, week 3-4: 40-50% off, final week: 60-80% off; (c) advertise clearance sale per W142 marketing campaigns; (d) items not sold during clearance: transfer to nearest BuildRight store or DC for redistribution; (e) unsaleable items (damaged, expired): write off per W91/W92; (f) final inventory count: physical count on last operating day for financial reconciliation | Merchandising Manager / Store Manager | VP Merchandising | 4-6 weeks |
| 4 | **Asset recovery**: Facilities Manager coordinates: (a) catalog all BuildRight-owned assets: POS terminals, racking, display fixtures, refrigeration units, paint mixing station, lumber cutting equipment, security cameras, IT equipment; (b) assess asset condition and recovery value: reusable (transfer to other locations or new store openings), sellable (sell to used equipment buyers), scrap (dispose per W443); (c) coordinate physical removal and logistics; (d) update fixed asset register per W184 and compute gain/loss on asset disposal; (e) ensure all assets removed before lease handover | Facilities Manager / Finance | COO | 2-4 weeks |
| 5 | **System & IT decommissioning**: CIO coordinates: (a) deactivate POS terminals and unregister from BIR per FIN-010; (b) decommission ERP location master per W508 (mark as inactive, not deleted for historical data retention); (c) deactivate user accounts for departing staff; (d) cancel internet/telecom service per W701; (e) retrieve IT equipment (terminals, handhelds, routers, printers); (f) ensure all data backed up per W256 retention policy; (g) wipe all devices of BuildRight data per W647 data privacy requirements | CIO | CIO | 1-2 weeks |
| 6 | **Regulatory closure filings**: Compliance Officer coordinates: (a) BIR branch closure registration: file closure notice with BIR Revenue District Office, surrender unused BIR-registered invoices, obtain Certificate of Registration cancellation; (b) LGU business permit cancellation; (c) DOLE closure report (if 50+ employees affected); (d) cancel DENR permits if applicable; (e) cancel local insurance policies; (f) notify PhilHealth, Pag-IBIG, SSS of employee separations | Compliance Officer | VP Legal | 1-2 months |
| 7 | **Lease handover**: Real Estate Manager coordinates: (a) restore premises to lease-required condition (remove BuildRight fixtures, repair walls, repaint if required by lease); (b) schedule landlord walk-through inspection; (c) negotiate security deposit return (typically 2-3 months rent); (d) settle any outstanding rent or utility balances; (e) formal lease termination agreement signed by both parties per W688; (f) utility disconnection and final billing per W701 | Real Estate Manager / Legal | COO | 2-4 weeks |
| 8 | **Financial close-out**: Finance Controller: (a) final P&L for closed store: revenue through closure date, all closure costs (employee separation, lease penalties, inventory write-off, asset disposal loss, removal costs); (b) compare closure costs to Board-approved budget; (c) capital gain/loss on leasehold improvements (if any); (d) update intercompany positions if store was in different entity per W442; (e) archive store financial records per W256 retention policy; (f) final closure report to CFO and Board | Finance Controller | CFO | 2-4 weeks |
| 9 | **Post-closure review**: VP Store Operations conducts post-closure review: (a) document lessons learned for future closures; (b) assess impact on customers in trade area: are they patronizing alternative BuildRight stores or switching to competitors?; (c) assess employee outcomes: how many transferred vs. separated?; (d) compare actual closure costs vs. budget; (e) update store portfolio strategy if closure was part of broader optimization | VP Store Operations | CEO | 2 weeks post-closure |

### System Touchpoints

- Project portfolio management (PPM) tool for closure task tracking
- ERP location master deactivation per W508
- Fixed asset register update per W184
- POS terminal de-registration per FIN-010
- HR separation processing per W643
- BIR closure filing per FIN-010
- Inventory management per W91/W92
- Customer communication per W708
- Lease management per W688
- Utility disconnection per W701
- Document retention per W256

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Employee litigation risk**: Philippine labor law provides strong employee protections; improper separation process (insufficient notice, below-minimum separation pay, failure to report to DOLE) can result in illegal dismissal claims with back wages and moral damages; mitigated by the VP Store Operations (project sponsor) review and approval gate and operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents
- **Inventory write-off magnitude**: if clearance sales do not move sufficient volume, remaining inventory must be transferred (logistics cost) or written off (financial impact); timing markdowns correctly is critical
- **Lease restoration costs**: landlords may demand extensive premises restoration beyond BuildRight's lease obligations; Legal must negotiate to limit restoration scope per lease terms
- **Community and brand impact**: store closures in provincial communities generate negative publicity and may affect BuildRight's reputation and employee morale at remaining locations
- **Customer migration failure**: customers of closed store may not migrate to alternative BuildRight locations, especially if competitor is closer; marketing must actively communicate alternative store locations per W708


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews lease termination provisions" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-document of "documents" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "count" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: VP Store Operations (project sponsor) review and approval gate; operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication

- **Closure Project Manager**: full-time during 2-4 month closure project. Can be the Store Opening Project Manager in dual role (rotating between openings and closures).
- **Store Manager**: full-time through closure (typically the last employee to leave).
- **Department resources**: ~5–10% of each department head's time during closure. Absorbed within existing roles.
- **No incremental headcount** (project-managed closure).

### Time Estimate

- Full closure project: 2-4 months from Board decision to lease handover
- Employee separation: 30-60 days
- Inventory liquidation: 4-6 weeks
- Asset recovery: 2-4 weeks
- System decommissioning: 1-2 weeks
- Regulatory filings: 1-2 months
- Lease handover: 2-4 weeks
- Financial close-out: 2-4 weeks
- Post-closure review: 2 weeks

## W742. Store-Level Key, Access Card & Secure Area Daily Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Employee onboarding (W15), separation (W43), shift start/end, lost key/card report |
| **Frequency** | Daily access transactions; periodic audits |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~29 employees × average 2 access credentials = ~11,600 active credentials |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Floor Associates, IT (W152), Security Guard, HR |

### Background

Each BuildRight Depot store manages physical keys, electronic access cards, alarm codes, and safe combinations for multiple secure areas: cash office, stockroom, IT/server room, pharmacy/chemical storage (per W236), and management office. With 200 stores and ~11,600 active credentials, systematic credential management is essential for security compliance and audit trail integrity.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Credential issuance — At onboarding (W15), Store Manager issues access card and/or physical keys based on role; system records issuance with employee ID, credential type, and access level | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 2 | Access level configuration — IT configures access card permissions per role: floor associate (sales floor, break room), stock associate (+ stockroom), cashier (+ cash office during shift), department supervisor (+ chemical storage), store manager (all areas) | IT | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | Shift-based access activation — At shift start, system activates access card for authorized areas; at shift end, system deactivates non-common areas | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Lost/stolen credential reporting — Employee reports lost key/card immediately; Store Manager deactivates access card in system and reissues; physical key loss triggers lock rekey for affected area | Employee / Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 5 | Quarterly combination change — Safe combination and alarm code changed quarterly; Store Manager updates system record and distributes new codes to authorized personnel via sealed envelope | Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | Separation credential collection — At offboarding (W43), Store Manager collects all physical keys and access cards; IT deactivates electronic access same day | Store Manager / IT | — | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly credential audit — Store Manager reconciles active credentials against current employee roster; flags unreturned credentials from separated employees | Store Manager | Store Manager | 1 hour |
| 8 | Security incident escalation — If unauthorized access detected (door forced, access at unusual hours), system triggers alert to Store Manager and LP (W37) | System / LP | LP | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Electronic access control system integration with employee master (W292)
- Role-based access level configuration linked to position/department
- Shift-based access activation/deactivation linked to W34 scheduling
- Credential audit report — active credentials vs. active employees
- LP alert integration (W37) for unauthorized access attempts
- IT provisioning integration (W152) for access card lifecycle

### Pain Points / Risks

- Unreturned credentials from separated employees — the #1 physical security gap in retail; monthly audit (Step 7) may not catch recently separated employees quickly enough
- Key duplication by employees — physical keys cannot be tracked like electronic cards; high-security areas should use electronic-only access
- Alarm code sharing among employees defeats individual accountability; biometric access for cash office and IT room mitigates this
- Seasonal workers (W555) create credential churn — 200 stores × seasonal hiring = significant issuance/collection volume during peak seasons


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-record of "records issuance with employee ID, credential type, and access level" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System rule-based authorization of "Shift-based access activation — At shift start, system activates" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-report of "reporting — Employee reports lost key/card immediately" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Time Estimate

Issuance: 10 min. Deactivation: 10 min. Lost credential: 30 min. Quarterly combination change: 30 min. Monthly audit: 1 hour.

### Staffing Implication

Store Manager absorbs credential management within existing duties. ~11,600 credentials across 200 stores requires systematic tracking. No incremental headcount, but access control system investment required for electronic access management.

---

## W826. Store-Level Layaway Payment Reminder & Forfeiture Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Layaway installment payment due per schedule created in W75; payment overdue > 7 days |
| **Frequency** | Weekly; ~100–200 overdue layaway accounts chain-wide at any time |
| **Volume** | ~100–200 overdue accounts/month; ~5–10% of active layaway plans reach forfeiture stage |
| **Owner** | CSR |
| **Participants** | CSR, Store Manager, Customer |

### Background

W75 covers layaway/installment sales creation but not the payment reminder and forfeiture sub-processes. BuildRight's layaway program (POS-020) allows customers to reserve high-value items (appliances, power tools, furniture) with a deposit and pay in installments over 30-90 days. When payments lapse, the store must: (a) proactively remind customers to avoid forfeiture, (b) process forfeiture per policy when payment deadlines are missed, (c) return forfeited items to selling stock, and (d) handle forfeited deposits per accounting policy (deposit forfeiture income vs. store credit refund). Philippine Consumer Act (RA 7394) requires fair forfeiture policies with adequate notice.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System generates daily overdue layaway report — categorized by aging bucket: (a) payment due in next 3 days (pre-due reminder queue), (b) 7 days overdue (first notice queue), (c) 14 days overdue (second notice/final notice queue), (d) 30 days overdue (forfeiture eligible queue); report includes: customer name, contact number, layaway plan number, item description, total plan value, payments made, balance due, overdue installment amount, days overdue | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Pre-due reminder** (3 days before payment due): system sends automated reminder to customer via W708 — SMS preferred channel (Philippine mobile penetration ~95%), email backup: (a) payment amount due, (b) due date, (c) payment options — in-store at any BuildRight, GCash per W99, bank transfer to BuildRight account, (d) consequence of missed payment: "If payment is not received by [date], your layaway may be subject to cancellation. Please contact us to discuss payment arrangements." | System / CSR | — | Automated (< 1 min) |
| 3 | **First overdue notice** (7 days overdue): CSR calls customer using contact number from layaway agreement: (a) confirms customer received pre-due reminder, (b) asks about payment intention — "When can you make your installment payment?", (c) offers one-time payment extension (max 14 additional days) with Store Manager approval for customers with good payment history (2+ on-time payments), (d) documents call outcome in system: "committed to pay by [date]", "requested extension", "unable to reach customer", "customer requests cancellation" | CSR | — | 5-10 min per call |
| 4 | **Final notice** (14 days overdue): CSR sends formal notice via W708 — registered SMS or email with read receipt: (a) states current overdue amount and total remaining balance, (b) warns: "This is your final notice. Per BuildRight layaway policy, your layaway plan will be cancelled if full overdue balance is not received within 14 days from this notice date [specific date].", (c) provides store contact number for payment arrangement, (d) explains forfeiture policy — deposit disposition per customer's election at plan creation | CSR | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 5 | **Forfeiture processing** (30 days overdue, grace period expired, no response or no payment): (a) Store Manager reviews layaway account — verifies customer contact history (all reminders and notices sent, call outcomes documented), approves forfeiture, (b) system cancels layaway agreement — layaway status changes to "Forfeited", (c) reserved inventory released back to available stock per POS-020 layaway inventory reservation — items return to sellable on-hand, (d) deposit forfeiture calculation per policy: Option A — full deposit forfeited (Dr. Cash / Cr. Forfeiture Income); Option B — deposit less cancellation fee (PHP 500 or 10% of deposit, whichever is higher) returned as store credit per W28 gift card/store credit issuance, (e) system posts forfeiture accounting entries — forfeiture income recognized, layaway liability cleared, inventory reservation released, (f) customer notified of forfeiture via W708 with deposit disposition explanation and store credit reference number if applicable | CSR / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15-20 min per forfeiture |
| 6 | **Partial payment layaway forfeiture**: if customer made some installments but forfeits: (a) calculate total payments made vs. total plan value, (b) apply forfeiture policy — typically all payments converted to store credit less cancellation fee (PHP 500 or 10% of total payments, whichever is higher), (c) store credit issued per W28 — can be used for any future purchase at any BuildRight store, (d) never refund cash for forfeited layaways per policy (deters strategic layaway abuse — customer using layaway to lock sale price then canceling), (e) accounting: Dr. Layaway Liability / Cr. Store Credit Liability (for credit issued) + Cr. Forfeiture Income (for cancellation fee retained) | CSR | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly layaway analytics report generated by system: (a) plan creation rate by store and by category, (b) completion rate — target >85% of plans completed without forfeiture, (c) forfeiture rate by store (flag stores >10% forfeiture for investigation), by category (flag categories >15% forfeiture for policy review), and by customer segment, (d) average plan duration (target: <60 days), (e) deposit forfeiture income — recognized monthly in GL, (f) payment reminder effectiveness — conversion rate from pre-due reminder to payment (target: >70%), first notice to payment (target: >50%), final notice to payment (target: >30%) | System | Store Manager / Finance | 2 hours/month review |

### System Touchpoints

- W75 layaway/installment sales — original plan creation with payment schedule and terms
- POS-020 layaway capability — inventory reservation, payment processing, plan lifecycle management
- W708 customer communication management — automated SMS/email reminders and formal notices
- W28 gift card/store credit — forfeiture store credit issuance for partial payment layaways
- W541 cash office — in-store layaway payment collection and reconciliation
- W99 payment settlement — GCash, bank transfer, and other digital payment methods for layaway installments
- W540 BIR receipt management — layaway payment receipts and forfeiture documentation
- W255 document storage — forfeiture approval documentation, customer communication logs
- W41 complaint resolution — customer disputes on forfeiture, deposit amount, or store credit calculation

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Customer disputes on forfeiture** — despite adequate notice (pre-due + first notice + final notice), customers may dispute forfeiture claiming they never received notices; mitigated by multi-channel notification (SMS + email + call) and system-logged contact history; W41 handles disputes
- **Deposit amount disputes at forfeiture** — customer disagrees with forfeiture calculation or cancellation fee; clear policy documentation at plan creation per W75 (signed acknowledgment of forfeiture terms) mitigates; Store Manager has authority to waive cancellation fee for goodwill on case-by-case basis
- **Strategic layaway abuse** — customer uses layaway to reserve sale-priced item (e.g., PHP 15,000 power tool on 20% off promo), then cancels when item returns to regular price or is restocked; "no cash refund on forfeiture" policy deters this behavior; system flag for repeat cancelers (>2 forfeitures in 12 months)
- **Inventory reservation impact** — items reserved for layaway are unavailable for other customers; if layaway customer forfeits after 30+ days, the item may have missed its selling window (seasonal items, promotional items); releasing reserved inventory promptly at forfeiture is critical
- **Forfeiture accounting complexity with partial payments** — when customer made 3 of 6 installments before forfeiting, the accounting requires splitting between store credit liability (for credit issued) and forfeiture income (for cancellation fee); automated system calculation per W826.6 reduces errors
- **Philippine Consumer Act compliance** — RA 7394 requires fair and transparent forfeiture practices; BuildRight policy must provide adequate notice (3 touchpoints over 30 days), reasonable cancellation fee, and documented customer acknowledgment of terms at plan creation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "generates daily overdue layaway report — categorized by aging bucket" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "account" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "documents call outcome in system" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: multi-channel notification (SMS + email + call) and system-logged contact history; operational: flag for repeat cancelers (>2 forfeitures in 12 months); operational: calculation per W826

### Time Estimate

Per pre-due reminder: automated (< 1 min). Per first notice call: 5-10 min. Per final notice: 5 min documentation. Per forfeiture processing: 15-20 min (including Store Manager review and approval). Monthly analytics review: 2 hours. Weekly chain-wide: ~100–200 contacts x 7 min avg = ~12–23 hours/week across all stores. Per store: ~4–7 minutes/week during active layaway periods.

### Staffing Implication

- Absorbed by existing CSR as part of customer service duties — layaway follow-up is a standard CSR responsibility
- Estimated ~12–23 hours/week chain-wide for layaway reminder and forfeiture processing
- Store Manager involvement: approval of extensions (5 min each), approval of forfeitures (5 min each), monthly analytics review (30 min); estimated ~1 hour/month
- Training on layaway policy, forfeiture procedures, and customer communication per W518 — 1-hour module for all CSRs
- No incremental headcount — layaway management is part of standard CSR workload

---

## W950. Store-Level Customer Vehicle Loading Assistance & Load Securing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases bulky or heavy items requiring assistance to load into their vehicle, or customer requests loading help at the yard/loading area |
| **Frequency** | ~40,000–60,000 loading events/month chain-wide (~200–300/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–10 items per loading event; average 3–4 items |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate (Loader), Customer, Receiving Clerk (yard coordination), Sales Associate (item retrieval) |

### Background

BuildRight's product mix includes many bulky, heavy, and oversized items (cement bags, plywood sheets, lumber, steel bars, tiles, appliances, water tanks, toilets) that customers cannot self-load. In the Philippine retail context, most customers transport purchases using personal vehicles (pickup trucks, SUVs, multicabs, motorcycles with sidecars) rather than professional delivery services. Proper loading assistance — including weight distribution, load securing with rope/straps, and vehicle weight limit advisory — is both a customer service imperative and a safety/liability concern. Improperly loaded vehicles can cause road accidents, damage to merchandise, and injury to BuildRight staff. This workflow formalizes the loading assistance process distinct from W438 (yard dispatch & loading operations, which covers commercial/professional loading) and W827 (load calculation & safety advisory, which is advisory-only). W950 covers the hands-on physical loading assistance service for retail customers.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Loading Request Initiation**: Customer completes purchase at POS; cashier identifies items requiring loading assistance: (a) heavy items (> 15kg per piece); (b) oversized items (length > 1.5m or volume > 0.1cbm); (c) fragile items (glass per W943, tiles, mirrors); (d) multi-item purchases (> 5 pieces); cashier prints loading slip with item list, quantities, and vehicle loading zone assignment; customer drives to designated loading zone (yard area for lumber/building materials, side door for general merchandise); Stock Associate notified via handheld of pending loading job | Cashier / System | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Vehicle Assessment & Load Planning**: Stock Associate meets customer at loading zone; assesses: (a) vehicle type — pickup truck (open bed), SUV/van, sedan, multicab, motorcycle/sidecar; (b) vehicle condition — tire condition, suspension, cargo capacity; (c) load plan — item placement sequence (heaviest items first/bottom, fragile items on top), weight distribution (left-right balance), and space optimization; for motorcycles with sidecars: max load 100kg total, items secured to sidecar frame only; for sedans: items inside cabin only (no trunk protrusion per LTO regulations); Stock Associate advises customer if vehicle cannot safely carry all items — offers: (a) split delivery (partial load now + delivery per W668 for remainder); (b) customer returns with larger vehicle; (c) expedited delivery scheduling | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Physical Loading Execution**: Stock Associate(s) load items into/onto vehicle: (a) uses proper lifting techniques (bent knees, straight back, team lift for items > 25kg per W697 ergonomics); (b) positions items per load plan; (c) protects vehicle surfaces with protective blankets/mats (BuildRight-provided); (d) protects merchandise with edge protection (cardboard corners for tiles/plywood); for yard items (lumber, cement, steel): Stock Associate uses yard forklift or hand truck per equipment safety protocol per W579; customer must remain in designated safe zone during forklift operations per W649; items that extend beyond vehicle length (lumber, pipes): Stock Associate attaches red flag per LTO regulations | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5–15 min |
| 4 | **Load Securing & Safety Verification**: Stock Associate secures load using: (a) ratchet straps (provided by BuildRight at cost); (b) nylon rope (available for purchase or complimentary for small loads); (c) bungee cords (customer-provided or available for purchase); verifies: (a) load stability — no shifting when vehicle gently rocked; (b) no protruding sharp edges; (c) clear driver visibility (no items blocking rear window or mirrors); (d) tailgate/hood secure; Stock Associate signs off on loading slip: "Load secured, safety verified"; customer countersigns acknowledging load acceptance and safe loading confirmation | Stock Associate / Customer | Store Manager | 3–5 min |
| 5 | **Post-Loading Documentation & Zone Cleanup**: Stock Associate returns loading slip to customer service counter; system records loading event for analytics (loading time, items, vehicle type, associate ID); loading zone cleared for next customer; loading equipment (hand trucks, forklift, blankets, straps) returned to storage; daily: Store Manager reviews loading log for safety incidents and service time metrics | Stock Associate / System | Store Manager | 2–3 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS loading slip generation for heavy/oversized items
- Handheld notification for Stock Associate dispatch
- Loading event logging for analytics and safety tracking
- Delivery scheduling integration (W668) for items exceeding vehicle capacity
- Forklift operation logging per W649 safety compliance
- Ergonomics incident tracking per W697
- Store KPI dashboard (W665) for loading service metrics

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer vehicle overloading**: Customers may insist on loading more than their vehicle can safely carry; Stock Associate must enforce safety limits with tact; documented refusal policy protects BuildRight from liability; offer delivery alternative per W668
- **Staff injury during loading**: Lifting heavy items is a primary injury risk; mandatory team-lift policy for items > 25kg; lifting equipment (dollies, hand trucks, forklift) must be available and functional; injury reporting per W140
- **Vehicle damage claims**: Customer may claim BuildRight staff scratched or dented vehicle during loading; loading zone CCTV per W838 provides evidence; protective blankets mitigate; liability disclaimer on loading slip
- **Peak period congestion**: Saturday mornings and payday weekends create loading zone congestion; dedicated loading time slots for large purchases; overflow parking for loaded vehicles awaiting departure
- **Motorcycle loading safety**: Many Filipino customers transport building materials on motorcycles/sidecars, which is extremely dangerous for heavy loads; Stock Associate trained to decline unsafe motorcycle loading and offer affordable delivery alternatives; documented refusal policy


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "prints loading slip with item list, quantities, and vehicle loading zone…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-flag of "flag per LTO regulations" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-count of "countersigns acknowledging load acceptance and safe loading confirmation" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: team-lift policy for items > 25kg

### Staffing Implication
- **Stock Associates**: Loading assistance is the primary non-replenishment activity for Stock Associates; at 200–300 loading events/store/month × 15–31 min per event (per the Time Estimate) = ~50–155 hours/month ≈ 2–5 hours/day; absorbed by existing 4 Stock Associates per store with rotating assignment
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Loading request initiation: 2–3 min
- Vehicle assessment & load planning: 3–5 min
- Physical loading: 5–15 min
- Load securing & safety verification: 3–5 min
- Documentation & cleanup: 2–3 min
- **Total per loading event**: 15–31 min of Stock Associate time

## W951. Store-Level Bulk Material Breaking & Custom Quantity Packaging

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a quantity smaller than the standard package/bundle for a bulk material (e.g., 5 individual cement bags from a pallet, 1 piece from a 10-pack, or 2 meters from a 50-meter roll) |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 transactions/month chain-wide (~40–60/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 line items per transaction |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer, Stock Associate (bulk area handling), Receiving Clerk |

### Background

Many construction materials are sold in bulk packaging (pallets of cement, bundles of rebar, rolls of wire, boxes of nails, cartons of tiles, sacks of aggregates). While professional contractors typically purchase full bundles, DIY customers and small-scale renovators often need smaller quantities. BuildRight's bulk breaking service — opening standard bulk packages and selling individual units or custom quantities — serves the significant DIY/retail customer segment (55% of revenue) and drives foot traffic from price-sensitive homeowners who might otherwise go to smaller neighborhood hardware stores (sari-sari stores). This is distinct from W463 (catch-weight & cut-to-length processing, which covers variable-weight/length items) and W439 (in-store bulk-to-retail repackaging, which covers proactive repackaging into standard retail units). W951 covers on-demand, customer-requested bulk breaking for items that are normally sold in standard packs but where the customer needs fewer than the standard quantity.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Request & Quantity Confirmation**: Customer requests quantity smaller than standard package; Sales Associate confirms: (a) item and standard package size (e.g., cement: sold per bag but stocked on pallet of 40; nails: sold per kg but stocked in 25kg boxes; tiles: sold per piece but stocked in cartons of 10–20); (b) requested quantity; (c) whether item is already available in broken quantities from previous W439 repackaging; (d) if not pre-broken, Sales Associate checks breakability policy — some items (sealed adhesives, safety equipment) cannot be broken from original packaging for safety/warranty reasons | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Bulk Break Execution**: Stock Associate retrieves full package from bulk storage area; performs breaking: (a) cement/lumber: individual units already separable — count and pull requested quantity; (b) nails/screws in bulk boxes: weigh requested quantity on scale, bag in plastic bag, label with weight and unit price; (c) wire/cable on rolls: measure and cut requested length; (d) tiles in cartons: open carton, count requested pieces, re-seal remaining carton with tape and update carton count label; (e) chemicals (paint, thinner, adhesive): decant into smaller container if policy allows, or sell minimum original container; Stock Associate creates a "broken pack" inventory adjustment in the system: original pack reduced by broken quantity; broken quantity created as individual unit(s) with same SKU | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–15 min |
| 3 | **Pricing & POS Processing**: System prices the broken quantity: (a) individual unit price from pricing master (W289) — same unit price as if sold individually; (b) if no individual price exists, system calculates pro-rata from pack price (pack price ÷ pack quantity); (c) no bulk-breaking surcharge (absorbed as customer service); customer pays at POS with broken items; system updates inventory: original pack reduced, individual units sold, remaining broken pack inventory available for next customer | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Broken Pack Inventory Management**: At end of day, Department Supervisor reviews broken pack inventory report: (a) partially broken packs that have been open > 7 days — assess for restocking (re-seal and return to shelf) or markdown (damaged packaging per W549); (b) broken pack accuracy — verify physical count matches system count; discrepancy investigated per W514; (c) fast-breaking items identified for proactive repackaging per W439 to reduce on-demand breaking time | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15–20 min/day |

### System Touchpoints
- POS with individual and pack pricing (W289 pricing master)
- Inventory adjustment module for broken pack creation
- Broken pack inventory aging report (7-day threshold)
- Inventory accuracy reconciliation (W514)
- Proactive repackaging trigger (W439) based on breaking frequency
- Catch-weight pricing (W463) for weight-based breaking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Inventory accuracy degradation**: Broken packs are a common source of inventory discrepancies; physical count may not match system due to miscounting, unlabeled partial packs, or pilferage; daily reconciliation per step 4 mitigates
- **Shelf space for partial packs**: Broken packages take irregular shelf space; dedicated "broken pack" section per department with clear labeling; partial packs with damaged packaging may appear unappealing to customers
- **Margin dilution**: Individual unit pricing may be lower per-unit than pack pricing; if cost is allocated on a pack basis, selling individual units at individual prices may reduce margin on remaining pack units; Category Manager reviews breakability and pricing per SKU quarterly
- **Waste from damaged packaging**: Opening sealed cartons/boxes generates packaging waste and may damage remaining items; Stock Associates trained in careful opening and re-sealing; damaged items dispositioned per W91


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "checks breakability policy — some items" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "count and pull requested quantity" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates pro-rata from pack price" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: due to miscounting, unlabeled partial packs, or pilferage

### Staffing Implication
- **Stock Associates**: Bulk breaking consumes ~0.5–1.2 hours/day at 40–60 events/month (incl. daily reconciliation); absorbed by existing Stock Associate role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Customer request & quantity confirmation: 3–5 min
- Bulk break execution: 5–15 min
- POS processing: 3–5 min
- Broken pack inventory management: 15–20 min/day
- **Total per transaction**: 11–25 min of staff time

## W952. Store-Level Customer Gratuity & Service Tip Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer offers a tip/gratuity to BuildRight staff for loading assistance (W950), delivery, cutting/fabrication services (W943–W946), or other exceptional service |
| **Frequency** | ~10,000–15,000 tip events/month chain-wide (~50–75/store/month) |
| **Volume** | PHP 20–200 per tip event (average ~PHP 50) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate/Stock Associate, Store Manager, HR/Payroll, Finance |

### Background

In Philippine retail culture, tipping for exceptional service (especially for physical labor like loading cement bags, cutting lumber, or delivering appliances) is common and culturally expected by many customers. Currently, tips are handled inconsistently — some associates accept, some decline, some pool, some keep individually. This creates: (a) inconsistent customer experience; (b) potential tax compliance issues (tips are taxable income per BIR); (c) fairness concerns among staff; (d) risk of associates soliciting tips or providing preferential service to tippers. BuildRight's formal gratuity management workflow provides a consistent, compliant, and fair framework for tip handling. This is not about encouraging tipping — BuildRight's service standard is excellent service for all customers — but about managing tips that customers voluntarily offer.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Service Delivery & Tip Offer**: Customer receives service (loading, cutting, delivery, etc.) and offers tip; the Sales Associate / Stock Associate follows policy: (a) initial response: politely declines ("It's our pleasure to help, sir/ma'am — no tip needed"); (b) if customer insists: accepts graciously with verbal thank-you; (c) never solicits tips or suggests tip amounts; (d) provides same quality service regardless of whether customer tips | Sales Associate / Stock Associate | Store Manager | 0 min (during service) |
| 2 | **Tip Recording & Deposit**: Associate records tip at end of shift: (a) cash tips: counts and records amount on daily tip log at customer service counter; (b) system entry: Associate enters tip amount in employee self-service portal with date, service type, and amount; (c) cash tips placed in sealed envelope with Associate's name and amount, deposited in store safe; tips held in safe until weekly processing; digital tips (if customer tips via digital payment — future capability) automatically recorded | Sales Associate / Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5 min/end of shift |
| 3 | **Weekly Tip Pooling & Distribution**: Weekly (every Monday): (a) Store Manager collects all tip envelopes from safe; (b) verifies amounts against daily tip logs; (c) applies tip pool distribution policy: Option A (individual): Associate keeps tips received for their service; Option B (team pool): all tips pooled and distributed equally among all front-line associates who worked that week; store selects one policy — recommend team pool for fairness; (d) system records distributed tip amounts per employee for payroll processing | Store Manager | HR/Payroll | 30 min/week |
| 4 | **Tax Withholding & Payroll Integration**: (a) tips are taxable compensation per BIR Revenue Regulations; (b) Payroll Officer includes weekly tip income in employee's semi-monthly payroll computation per W10; (c) tips added to taxable income for withholding tax computation per TRAIN law tables; (d) tips included in SSS, PhilHealth, Pag-IBIG contribution base per statutory requirements; (e) net tip amount (after withholding) added to employee's payroll credit; (f) pay slip shows tip income separately for transparency | Payroll Officer / System | Payroll Manager | Automated per W10 |
| 5 | **Monthly Tip Analytics & Policy Review**: Store Manager reviews: (a) total tip amount by store, department, and associate; (b) tip frequency and average amount by service type; (c) correlation between tip activity and customer satisfaction scores per W608; (d) any associates with tip amounts significantly above/below peers — investigated for potential preferential service or tip solicitation; (e) quarterly: HR reviews tip policy effectiveness and fairness; adjusts pool distribution method if needed | Store Manager / HR | CHRO | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Employee self-service portal for tip recording
- Store safe management module for tip envelope tracking
- Payroll system integration (W10) for tax withholding and statutory contributions
- Tip analytics dashboard in BI module
- Customer satisfaction correlation (W608)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Tax compliance**: Failure to report tips as taxable income exposes BuildRight and employees to BIR penalties; automated payroll integration per step 4 ensures compliance; employee communication that tips are included in taxable income
- **Tip solicitation risk**: Associates may subtly solicit tips or provide better service to customers who tip; Store Manager monitoring per step 5 and loss prevention awareness per W755 mitigate; disciplinary action for confirmed solicitation per W603
- **Cash handling risk**: Cash tips in store safe create additional cash handling exposure; sealed envelope system with dual verification (associate + Store Manager) per W541 controls; tip amounts relatively small (PHP 50 average) limit exposure
- **Team morale impact**: Individual tip keeping creates inequity (loading associates receive more tips than cashiers); team pool recommended for BuildRight to promote teamwork; pool policy communicated during onboarding per W609


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters tip amount in employee self-service portal with date, service type, and…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "Weekly Tip Pooling & Distribution" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-review of "Monthly Tip Analytics & Policy Review" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: with dual verification (associate + Store Manager) per W541 controls

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~30 min/week on tip collection and distribution; absorbed by existing role
- **Payroll Officer**: minimal incremental effort (automated per W10)
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Tip recording: 5 min/end of shift per associate
- Weekly pooling & distribution: 30 min/week
- Tax & payroll integration: automated
- Monthly analytics: 30 min/month
- **Total weekly store effort**: ~60–90 min

## W958. Store-Level Daily Cash Variance Threshold Monitoring & Exception Escalation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | POS end-of-day closing process detects cash variance exceeding predefined thresholds |
| **Frequency** | ~200–400 variance events/month chain-wide (1–2 per store/month exceeding threshold) |
| **Volume** | 1 POS terminal per event; variance range PHP 50–5,000+ |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Manager, Loss Prevention Officer, Finance (Cash Office), Regional Manager (high-variance escalation) |

### Background

Cash variance — the difference between POS system-recorded cash and physical cash counted — is an inevitable reality in high-volume retail with 2.8M monthly transactions. BuildRight processes ~42% of transactions in cash (PHP 2.1B/month cash sales), making cash management a critical control point. Existing workflows cover individual aspects: W541 (cash office operations), W764 (daily opening safe count), W522 (daily transaction review), W272 (cashier over/short dispute), and W842 (cash handling exception monitoring). W958 fills the gap between these workflows by establishing a comprehensive, automated daily monitoring system with predefined thresholds, automated escalation, and investigation protocols. This workflow provides the "glue" that connects existing cash management workflows into a unified daily monitoring discipline.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated Variance Detection**: At POS end-of-day closing per W574: (a) system calculates cash variance per terminal: (system expected cash from Z-read) minus (physical cash count by cashier + Store Manager dual count); (b) system applies variance thresholds: (i) Green: ≤ PHP 50 absolute variance — acceptable, no action; (ii) Yellow: PHP 50.01–200 — acceptable, logged for trending; (iii) Orange: PHP 200.01–1,000 — requires cashier explanation and Store Manager investigation; (iv) Red: > PHP 1,000 — mandatory investigation, LP Officer notification, and Regional Manager alert; (c) system automatically categorizes each terminal's variance and sends alerts per threshold level | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Cashier Explanation & Store Manager Investigation**: For Orange and Red variances: (a) Cashier completes Variance Explanation Form (digital, in POS system): (i) variance amount (over or short); (ii) possible cause (customer rush, no time to count change, system error, counterfeit bill received, multi-tender confusion, POS malfunction); (b) Store Manager investigates: (i) reviews transaction log for suspicious voids, refunds, or discounts per W538; (ii) checks CCTV at checkout area per W838 for visible incidents; (iii) interviews cashier (brief, non-accusatory per W562 LP protocol); (iv) reviews cashier's variance history (rolling 30-day trend per cashier ID); (v) checks if variance coincides with shift handover per W517 | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15–30 min |
| 3 | **Resolution & Documentation**: Store Manager determines cause: (a) identified cause — system error, cashier mistake, counterfeit bill, POS glitch: (i) documents cause and corrective action in variance log; (ii) if cashier error: coaching per W518 POS training; recurring errors → retraining mandate per W522; (iii) if counterfeit: logs per W842 LP protocol, notifies bank per W89; (b) unidentified cause: (i) variance logged as "unexplained"; (ii) LP Officer reviews per W837 exception-based reporting within 48 hours; (iii) if unexplained variance persists for same cashier over 3 occurrences in 30 days → LP investigation per W839 internal theft protocol; (c) all variances posted to GL: overs deposited to store cash over account; shorts charged to Store Manager's cash accountability ledger (recoverable per company policy after investigation) | Store Manager / LP Officer | Regional Manager | 10–20 min |
| 4 | **Automated Trending & Predictive Alerting**: System continuously analyzes: (a) per-cashier variance trend (rolling 30-day); (b) per-store aggregate variance trend (rolling 30-day); (c) per-terminal variance pattern (may indicate hardware issue); (d) system generates predictive alerts: (i) "Cashier X has 3+ Yellow variances in 7 days — trending toward Orange"; (ii) "Store Y aggregate cash variance exceeds 0.05% of monthly cash sales — above chain average"; (iii) "Terminal Z shows consistent small shorts (PHP 10–30) — possible calibration issue"; alerts pushed to Store Manager and LP Officer for proactive intervention | System | LP Manager / Regional Manager | Automated |
| 5 | **Monthly Cash Variance Reporting**: (a) Finance generates monthly cash variance report per store: total overs, total shorts, net variance, % of cash sales, investigation outcomes; (b) LP reviews chain-wide trending: top 10 stores by variance rate, top 10 cashiers by variance frequency, terminal-specific patterns; (c) quarterly: Finance reviews and adjusts variance thresholds based on actual performance data; (d) annual: Internal Audit includes cash variance monitoring in store audit per W121 operational audit | Finance / LP Manager / Internal Audit | CFO / VP Store Operations | 4–6 hours/month (Finance + LP) |

### System Touchpoints
- POS end-of-day Z-read with automatic variance calculation
- Variance threshold engine with configurable Green/Yellow/Orange/Red levels
- Cashier Variance Explanation Form (digital, integrated with POS)
- CCTV integration per W838 for investigation support
- Transaction log analysis per W538 for suspicious activity detection
- Cashier variance trending module (30-day rolling)
- Store aggregate variance dashboard
- Terminal-specific variance analytics
- LP investigation module (W837, W839, W842)
- GL posting for cash over/short
- BI dashboard for chain-wide variance trending
- Alert/notification system for threshold breaches

### Pain Points / Risks
- **False positives**: Automated threshold alerts may generate excessive Orange/Red alerts that overwhelm Store Managers; threshold calibration per step 5c ensures alerts are meaningful; start with conservative thresholds and tighten over time based on data
- **Cashier morale**: Frequent variance investigations may create anxiety among honest cashiers; approach must be supportive (coaching) not punitive; distinguish between training issues (honest mistakes) and behavioral issues (theft indicators)
- **Cash counting accuracy**: Dual-count protocol (cashier + Store Manager) adds time to closing process but improves accuracy; electronic cash counters (per W212 Smart Safe) at top 50 stores automate counting and reduce variance
- **System vs. physical variance**: Some variances are caused by POS system issues (incorrect denomination entry, offline transaction reconciliation errors per W535) rather than cashier error; system error variances are not held against cashiers
- **Threshold gaming**: Sophisticated cash handlers may intentionally keep variances just below threshold to avoid detection; trending analysis per step 4 catches this pattern (consistent small shorts); LP Officer trained on threshold-gaming detection per W842


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "logged for trending" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-review of "reviews transaction log for suspicious voids, refunds, or discounts per W538" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-document of "Resolution & Documentation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Store Manager review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~15–30 min/day on variance investigation for Orange/Red events; absorbed by existing role (part of daily closing routine per W574)
- **LP Officer**: ~1–2 hours/week on variance trend analysis and investigation support for high-variance stores; absorbed by existing regional LP team (20 Officers)
- **Finance**: ~2–3 hours/month on chain-wide variance reporting; absorbed by existing team
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Automated detection: 0 min (automated)
- Cashier explanation: 5–10 min
- Store Manager investigation: 15–30 min
- Resolution & documentation: 10–20 min
- Monthly reporting: 4–6 hours/month (chain-wide)
- **Total per variance event**: 30–60 min of staff time
