## W1419. Store-Level Daily Sales Flash Report Generation & HQ Consolidation Dashboard Update

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store end-of-day (EOD) system close; daily at each store's closing time (typically 8:00–9:00 PM) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store; consolidated at HQ by 10:00 AM next business day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 365 days = 73,000 daily flash reports/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (store-level); VP Store Operations (HQ consolidation) |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Cashier, VP Store Operations, FP&A Analyst, VP Merchandising, CEO |

### Background

At BuildRight Depot's scale (200 stores, ~PHP 5.19B monthly revenue), daily visibility into store-level sales performance is critical for operational decision-making. The daily sales flash report serves multiple stakeholders: (a) Store Managers need to track their store's daily performance against target, (b) VP Store Operations needs chain-wide visibility to identify underperforming stores immediately, (c) VP Merchandising needs category-level sell-through data to adjust promotions and allocations, (d) CEO and CFO need consolidated daily revenue for cash flow management per W37, (e) FP&A Analyst uses daily flash data to refine monthly forecasts. Unlike detailed weekly/monthly reporting (W67, W575), the daily flash focuses on speed (generated within 1 hour of store close) and brevity (top-line KPIs only). This workflow manages the automated generation, distribution, and escalation of daily sales flash reports.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated store-level flash generation** (at store close): System automatically generates daily flash per store upon Z-report completion per W574: (a) total revenue (gross), (b) number of transactions, (c) average transaction value (ATV), (d) top 10 selling items by revenue, (e) category breakdown (% of daily sales by category), (f) returns/voids count and value, (g) payment method split (cash/card/e-wallet), (h) vs. daily target (system compares to plan — daily target = monthly plan ÷ operating days), (i) vs. same day last year (YoY comparison) | System | Store Manager | Automated (< 5 min) |
| 2 | **Store Manager review**: Store Manager reviews flash report on store manager mobile app: (a) verify revenue vs. daily target — if below 80% of target, Store Manager must add commentary (foot traffic observation, weather, local events), (b) review ATV — if significantly below PHP 1,800, may indicate price scanning issues or customer mix shift, (c) acknowledge receipt in system (tracks store-level compliance) | Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **HQ overnight consolidation** (by 6:00 AM next day): System consolidates all 200 store flash reports: (a) chain-wide total revenue, transactions, ATV, (b) regional breakdown (Mindanao, Visayas, Luzon, NCR, North/Central Luzon), (c) top 10 stores by revenue, (d) bottom 10 stores by variance-to-target, (e) category performance (which categories are trending above/below plan for the day), (f) BOPIS and ecommerce contribution to daily revenue, (g) cash/card/e-wallet split chain-wide | System | VP Store Operations | Automated (< 30 min) |
| 4 | **Morning executive review** (by 10:00 AM): (a) VP Store Operations reviews consolidated flash: identify stores below 80% of target — email flag to Regional Manager for follow-up, (b) FP&A Analyst reviews daily revenue run-rate: are we tracking to monthly plan? calculate implied monthly revenue based on actual days elapsed + projected remaining days, (c) VP Merchandising reviews category performance: identify categories significantly above/below daily plan — trigger action (promotion boost, allocation change), (d) CEO receives one-page daily flash summary via mobile app | VP Store Operations / FP&A Analyst / VP Merchandising | CEO | 15–30 min |
| 5 | **Exception escalation**: (a) stores below 70% of daily target for 2+ consecutive days: Regional Manager must visit store within 48 hours and report root cause, (b) stores with ATV drop > 20% vs. same day last year: Loss Prevention team reviews for potential POS manipulation per W562, (c) chain-wide daily revenue below 90% of plan for 3+ consecutive days: VP Store Operations convenes emergency review with Merchandising, Marketing, and FP&A | Regional Manager / VP Store Operations | COO | As needed |

### System Touchpoints
- Automated daily flash engine: generates store-level report from POS Z-report data (W1419.1)
- Store manager mobile app: flash report delivery with acknowledge/commentary feature (W1419.2)
- HQ consolidation dashboard: chain-wide aggregation with regional, category, and YoY breakdowns (W1419.3)
- Executive mobile app: one-page daily summary for CEO/CFO (W1419.4)
- Exception alerting engine: automated escalation triggers based on threshold rules (W1419.5)
- Integration with W574 (store daily closing — Z-report data source), W575 (weekly sales review — uses flash data for weekly aggregation), W67 (monthly store performance review — uses flash data for monthly roll-up), W37 (treasury cash forecast — daily revenue input), W665 (store KPI dashboard — same data source)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Store non-compliance with acknowledgment**: Store Managers may not acknowledge flash reports promptly, delaying HQ awareness of issues; mitigated by tracking acknowledgment compliance in Store Manager KPI scorecard
- **Data latency for late-closing stores**: stores in different time zones or with extended hours may not submit Z-reports until after HQ consolidation deadline; mitigated by running a second consolidation at noon with updated data
- **Flash report fatigue**: daily reporting can become noise if not action-oriented; mitigated by exception-based escalation (only flag variances, not routine results)
- **System dependency**: if POS near-real-time sync fails per W525, flash data will be incomplete; mitigated by offline data buffering and next-day reconciliation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "count and value" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scanning issues or customer mix shift" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-report of "HQ overnight consolidation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: tracking acknowledgment compliance in Store Manager KPI scorecard; operational: running a second consolidation at noon with updated data; operational: exception-based escalation (only flag variances, not routine results)

### Time Estimate
- Store Manager: 5–10 min/day per store = ~17–33 hours/day chain-wide (absorbed in daily routine)
- VP Store Operations: 15–30 min/day for consolidated review
- FP&A Analyst: 15 min/day for run-rate analysis
- Total HQ effort: ~45–60 min/day = ~275–365 hours/year

### Staffing Implication
- Fully automated at store level (system-generated from POS data)
- Store Manager: 5–10 min/day acknowledged in existing daily duties
- VP Store Operations: 15–30 min/day. Absorbed.
- FP&A Analyst: 15 min/day. Absorbed.
- No incremental headcount.

---

## W1485. Store Paint Mixing Station Daily Calibration, Color Formula Database Update & Tint Inventory Replenishment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of each store operating day; also triggered when new color formula batch is received from HQ Merchandising or tint cartridge stock falls below reorder point |
| **Frequency** | Daily calibration; weekly formula update; replenishment as needed |
| **Volume** | 1 calibration per store per day (200/day chain-wide); ~4–6 formula updates/month; ~2–3 tint replenishment orders/store/month |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Paint & Decor) |
| **Participants** | Paint Desk Associate, Department Supervisor, Merchandise Planner (HQ) |

### Background

BuildRight stores operate a paint mixing station where associates tint base paints to customer-specified colors using automated colorant dispensers. Each store mixes approximately 80–120 custom paint batches per month (~3–4 per day, per W1282), within PHP 15,000–30,000 in daily paint department revenue. Accurate color matching depends on: (a) daily calibration of the colorant dispensing machine to ensure correct volumes of each tint, (b) an up-to-date color formula database aligned with supplier paint lines (Boysen, Davies, Handyman, Nippon Paint), and (c) adequate stock of tint cartridges (typically 12–16 colorants per station). A miscalibrated machine produces off-color paint requiring costly remakes (PHP 800–2,500 per incident), while outdated formulas lead to customer complaints when mixed colors do not match displayed fan decks or competitor samples.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily colorant dispenser calibration**: At store opening: (a) Paint Desk Associate runs automated calibration cycle on the tint dispenser; (b) system dispenses measured drops of each of the 12–16 colorants into a calibration vial; (c) vial weight compared to expected weight per colorant; (d) variance > 2% triggers recalibration or cartridge replacement; (e) calibration results logged in system with date, time, and associate ID; (f) if calibration fails after 2 attempts: station flagged as "Calibration Required — Manual Override" and Department Supervisor notified | Paint Desk Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min |
| 2 | **Tint cartridge inventory check**: After calibration: (a) system reads current fill levels of all tint cartridges from dispenser sensors; (b) cartridges below 20% capacity flagged for reorder; (c) Paint Desk Associate visually confirms cartridge levels for accuracy; (d) replenishment order auto-generated per min/max rules; (e) critical colorants (white, black, red oxide, yellow oxide) that reach < 10% trigger urgent reorder with next-day delivery priority | Paint Desk Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | **Color formula database sync**: When HQ Merchandising releases updated formulas: (a) system downloads new formula batch to store paint mixing terminal; (b) formulas include new supplier color collections, seasonal specials, and corrected formulas from quality feedback; (c) Paint Desk Associate verifies download completion and confirms formula count matches HQ notification; (d) old formulas archived but remain accessible for 90 days for warranty re-matches; (e) test mix of one random new formula to verify output accuracy | System / Paint Desk Associate | Merchandise Planner | 5 min |
| 4 | **Customer color match verification**: When a customer brings a physical sample (chip, swatch, object): (a) Paint Desk Associate uses the spectrophotometer to scan the sample; (b) system matches to nearest formula in the database; (c) associate mixes a 50 ml test sample; (d) test sample compared to customer's sample under the station's daylight-balanced light; (e) if acceptable: full gallon mixed per formula; (f) if not acceptable: associate manually adjusts formula by eye/experience, re-tests; (g) final formula saved as custom mix in customer's profile for future reorder | Paint Desk Associate | Department Supervisor | 8–15 min per match |
| 5 | **End-of-day tint usage reconciliation**: At store closing: (a) system generates daily tint usage report showing volume dispensed per colorant; (b) actual cartridge level readings vs. calculated usage compared; (c) variance > 5% flagged for investigation (possible leak, evaporation, or dispenser malfunction); (d) daily paint revenue vs. gallons mixed cross-checked for average selling price monitoring | System / Paint Desk Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 6 | **Weekly paint station deep clean & maintenance**: Weekly: (a) Paint Desk Associate performs deep cleaning of tint dispenser nozzles, mixing area, and spectrophotometer lens; (b) expired or contaminated base paint inventory pulled per PA-05.3 disposition rules; (c) fan deck displays cleaned and damaged/missing cards replaced; (d) any dispenser maintenance issues reported per PA-20.3 equipment maintenance workflow | Paint Desk Associate | Department Supervisor | 30 min/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Automated colorant dispenser with sensor-based fill-level monitoring and calibration logging (W1485.1–2)
- Color formula database with HQ-to-store sync and version management (W1485.3)
- Spectrophotometer integration for customer color scanning and matching (W1485.4)
- Tint cartridge inventory min/max with auto-replenishment order generation (W1485.2)
- Daily tint usage reconciliation report with variance alerting (W1485.5)
- Customer custom paint formula storage in CRM profile for reorder (W1485.4)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Calibration drift in tropical humidity**: Philippine climate (60–90% humidity) can affect colorant viscosity, requiring more frequent calibration than temperate climates; some stores in coastal areas report faster drift
- **Multiple paint supplier ecosystems**: each supplier (Boysen, Davies, Nippon) uses different colorant systems and formula structures; maintaining accurate cross-references is complex
- **Tint stock-out impact**: a single missing colorant cartridge (e.g., raw umber) can prevent mixing 30–40% of popular colors; critical stock management essential
- **Spectrophotometer accuracy on textured surfaces**: customers often bring textured materials (wood, concrete, tile) that produce inconsistent readings; associate skill in manual adjustment remains important


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "logged in system with date, time, and associate ID" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Tint cartridge inventory check" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "count matches HQ notification" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: periodic reconciliation against source documents

### Staffing Implication
- Paint Desk Associate: ~30 min/day on calibration + inventory + reconciliation. Absorbed within existing paint desk staffing.
- Department Supervisor: ~15 min/day on oversight + 30 min/week on deep clean. Absorbed.
- No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate
- Daily routine (calibration + check + reconciliation): ~20 min/store/day
- Color match per customer: 8–15 min
- Weekly deep clean: 30 min
- Formula update: 5 min (occasional)
- **Annual effort per store: ~130 hours + ~600 color matches × 12 min avg = ~250 hours/store/year**

---


## W1490. Store-Level Plumbing & Electrical Fixture Display Model Rotation, Demo Unit Tracking & Write-Off

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly display refresh cycle; also triggered when display model is damaged, vendor launches new product line, or customer purchases display unit |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly major rotation; ad-hoc replacements as needed; ~2–5 demo unit write-offs per store per quarter |
| **Volume** | ~30–50 display fixture SKUs per store (faucets, toilets, showerheads, light fixtures, ceiling fans, switches); ~200 stores |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Visual Merchandising Associate, Department Supervisor, Loss Prevention Associate, Merchandise Planner |

### Background

BuildRight stores display 30–50 plumbing and electrical fixture models on the showroom floor — working faucet displays, toilet displays, shower enclosures, ceiling fan demos, and light fixture galleries. These display units serve as the primary sales tool for customers evaluating fixtures for home renovation projects. Display units are not regular sellable inventory: they are sample units that may be provided free or at deep discount by vendors, they suffer wear from customer handling (faucets turned on/off hundreds of times, ceiling fans running continuously, toilet seats tested by children), and they eventually become unsellable due to cosmetic damage, missing parts, or model discontinuation. Managing the lifecycle of display units — from initial setup through rotation to write-off — is essential for maintaining showroom appeal while controlling display costs.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Display model inventory tagging**: Each display unit: (a) tagged with a "Display Model — Not for Sale" label and unique display asset number; (b) entered in system as display inventory (separate from sellable stock) with: original cost, vendor-provided flag (free/discounted/purchased), installation date, and expected rotation date; (c) linked to the corresponding sellable SKU so customers can scan QR code on display to see pricing and stock availability; (d) display assets tracked separately from regular inventory for cost accounting | Visual Merchandising Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min per unit |
| 2 | **Quarterly display rotation assessment**: Quarterly: (a) Department Supervisor reviews all display units: physical condition, model currency (is it still in the active catalog?), vendor display allowance status, customer engagement (measured by QR scans per display); (b) units rated as: (i) "Keep" — good condition, current model, high engagement; (ii) "Rotate" — worn or outdated, replace with new model; (iii) "Sell" — good condition but model being discontinued, offer at 20–30% discount to clearance customers; (iv) "Write-off" — damaged, missing parts, unsellable; (c) rotation plan submitted to Merchandise Planner for new model allocation | Department Supervisor | Merchandise Planner | 2–3 hours/quarter |
| 3 | **Display unit write-off processing**: For units marked "Write-off": (a) Department Supervisor initiates write-off request in system with photos of condition and reason code (customer damage, normal wear, vendor defect, weather damage); (b) write-off value = original cost less any vendor display allowance already received; (c) LP Associate performs spot verification for write-offs > PHP 5,000 to rule out theft; (d) Department Supervisor approves write-offs < PHP 10,000; Store Manager approves PHP 10,000–50,000; VP Merchandising approves > PHP 50,000; (e) write-off posted to display expense account (not COGS); (f) physical unit disposed of or returned to vendor for credit per vendor agreement | Department Supervisor / LP Associate | Store Manager | 15–30 min per write-off |
| 4 | **Customer purchase of display unit**: When customer wants to buy the display model: (a) Sales Associate checks if model is still in active catalog and sellable stock is available; (b) if stock available: encourage customer to buy new-in-box at regular price; (c) if stock unavailable or customer insists on display: apply display unit discount (typically 20–40% off); (d) display unit removed from display asset register and sold as a regular transaction with "Display Unit — Sold As-Is" notation; (e) replacement display unit ordered from next replenishment cycle; (f) gap on display wall covered with "New Model Coming Soon" signage | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Display asset register separate from sellable inventory with vendor allowance tracking (W1490.1)
- QR code generation linked to sellable SKU with real-time stock availability (W1490.1)
- Display rotation assessment module with condition rating and disposition recommendation (W1490.2)
- Write-off workflow with tiered approval and LP verification trigger (W1490.3)
- Display-to-sale conversion transaction with "As-Is" flag and automatic discount (W1490.4)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Display cost accumulation**: with 30–50 display units per store at PHP 1,000–15,000 each, total display investment is PHP 50K–750K per store; quarterly rotation can cost PHP 10K–50K per store unless vendors provide free samples
- **Customer handling damage**: in high-traffic stores, faucet handles break, toilet lids crack, and light fixtures are scratched within 2–3 months; some customers treat the showroom as a playground
- **Vendor display allowance tracking**: vendors like Kohler, Grohe, and Siemens/ABB provide free or subsidized display units, but only if BuildRight meets display compliance requirements (positioning, signage, lighting); non-compliance results in vendor charging full price
- **QR code scan data underutilization**: display engagement data from QR scans could inform assortment decisions, but currently only 5–10% of customers scan the codes


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "entered in system as display inventory" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scans per display" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "Display unit write-off processing" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: Department Supervisor review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- Visual Merchandising Associate: ~10 min per unit setup × 30–50 units = ~5–8 hours/quarter for rotation. Absorbed.
- Department Supervisor: ~3 hours/quarter on rotation assessment + 15 min per write-off × ~3/quarter. Absorbed.
- LP Associate: ~30 min per high-value write-off verification × ~2/quarter. Absorbed.
- No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate
- Quarterly rotation: ~8–12 hours/store
- Write-off processing: ~1–2 hours/store/quarter
- Ad-hoc replacements: ~1 hour/store/month
- **Annual: ~50–70 hours/store/year**

---

## W1506. Customer Non-Stock / Special Order Full Lifecycle Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a product not carried in the store's regular assortment (discontinued, specialty item, bulk quantity beyond shelf stock, or custom specification); or Sales Associate identifies unfulfilled customer need during project consultation per W148 |
| **Frequency** | ~15–25 special orders per store per month |
| **Volume** | ~3,000–5,000 special orders/month chain-wide; average order value PHP 8,000–25,000 (higher than regular POS transactions) |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative (Store) |
| **Participants** | CSR, Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Category Manager (HQ), Buyer (HQ), Vendor, Receiving Clerk, Cashier, Customer |

### Background

BuildRight Depot carries 35,000 active SKUs but the total SKU master includes ~55,000 items — 20,000 are discontinued, seasonal, or specialty items not regularly stocked. Additionally, customers (particularly trade professionals and project buyers) frequently request items outside BuildRight's assortment: specialty tiles, custom hardware, large-quantity bulk orders, imported fixtures, or made-to-order items. The profile defines "Non-Stock / Special Order" as items ordered on demand for the customer. While W1133 covers cancellation and partial refund of special orders, no workflow covers the complete lifecycle from customer request through sourcing, ordering, fulfillment, and delivery. Non-stock orders represent ~2–3% of revenue but carry significantly higher margins (35–45%) because customers are willing to pay premium for hard-to-find items and BuildRight avoids the carrying cost of slow-moving inventory.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer request capture**: Customer approaches Sales Associate or CSR with product request; Associate captures: (a) product description and specifications (dimensions, material, color, brand, model), (b) quantity required, (c) required-by date, (d) customer contact details, (e) whether the item is for a specific project with deadline; Associate searches ERP for existing SKU match (may be discontinued or at another store); if no match found, create Special Order Request (SOR) in system | Sales Associate / CSR | Dept Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Sourcing**: CSR or Department Supervisor identifies potential vendor: (a) check existing vendor master for suppliers of the product category, (b) if new vendor required, initiate expedited vendor creation per MDM-008 (minimum: TIN, contact, payment terms), (c) contact vendor for price quote, lead time, and minimum order quantity; for import items: confirm customs classification, estimated landed cost per FIN-013, and import lead time (45–90 days); (d) if multiple vendors available: select based on price, lead time, and reliability score per W959 | CSR / Category Manager | Dept Supervisor | 1–4 hours |
| 3 | **Customer quotation**: CSR prepares quotation for customer: (a) item cost + markup (standard special order margin: 35–45%), (b) estimated lead time, (c) payment terms: 50% deposit at order placement (non-refundable), balance at pickup/delivery, (d) cancellation policy: deposit forfeited if vendor has already been ordered; CSR contacts customer with quotation via phone, SMS, or email | CSR | Dept Supervisor | 15–30 min |
| 4 | **Order confirmation & deposit collection**: Customer accepts quotation: (a) system creates Sales Order with "Special Order" type flag, (b) CSR collects 50% deposit at POS against the special order; deposit linked to Sales Order in ERP, (c) system auto-generates Purchase Requisition routed to Category Manager for approval, (d) Category Manager approves PR and converts to Purchase Order; PO includes: vendor, item description, quantity, required-by date, delivery location (store or DC), (e) customer receives order confirmation SMS/email with SO number and estimated availability date | CSR / Cashier / Category Manager | Store Manager | 20–30 min |
| 5 | **PO tracking & vendor follow-up**: CSR monitors PO status weekly: (a) system auto-tracks PO status: Sent → Acknowledged → In Production → Shipped → Received, (b) if vendor misses acknowledged ship date: CSR contacts vendor for update and revised ETA, (c) for import POs: track customs clearance status per PA-02.2, (d) if ETA will miss customer's required-by date: CSR contacts customer immediately with revised date and option to cancel per W1133 | CSR | Category Manager | 10 min/week per open order |
| 6 | **Goods receipt**: Vendor delivers special order to store: (a) Receiving Clerk processes goods receipt against PO per PUR-011; verifies product matches description, quantity, and condition, (b) for custom or specification-critical items: Department Supervisor performs quality inspection against customer specifications, (c) system updates Sales Order status to "Available for Pickup"; (d) auto-generate customer notification: SMS + email "Your special order is ready for pickup" with store location and 10-day hold period | Receiving Clerk / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 15–30 min |
| 7 | **Customer pickup & balance payment**: Customer arrives to collect: (a) CSR retrieves item from secure special order staging area, (b) Cashier collects remaining 50% balance at POS, (c) system closes Sales Order, recognizes revenue, (d) for trade accounts: charge to account per W1269 credit terms | CSR / Cashier | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 8 | **Unclaimed order management**: If customer does not pick up within 10-day hold period: (a) CSR makes 2 reminder calls (Day 5 and Day 8), (b) after Day 10: item disposition options — (i) return to vendor if returnable (vendor return policy permitting per PUR-012), (ii) add to store regular inventory if item fits assortment, (iii) mark down per W93 if close to assortment but slow-moving, (c) deposit handling per W1133 cancellation policy; (d) if item added to regular inventory: deposit credited to store miscellaneous income | CSR / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min per unclaimed order |

### System Touchpoints

- Special Order Request (SOR) creation module linked to customer record and item inquiry
- Vendor master and procurement module for sourcing and PO creation per PUR-001
- Deposit management at POS with Sales Order linkage
- PO status tracking with automated milestone notifications
- Goods receipt processing linked to Sales Order availability trigger
- Customer notification engine (SMS + email) for order milestones
- Unclaimed order aging report with auto-escalation
- Special order analytics dashboard: order count, fill rate, lead time, margin, cancellation rate

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Long lead times lose customers**: customers needing items for active construction projects cannot wait 45–90 days for import special orders; they will source from competitors who carry the item in stock; mitigated by transparent lead time communication at quotation (Step 3) and suggesting in-stock alternatives where possible
- **Vendor reliability for one-off orders**: vendors prioritize regular replenishment orders over one-time special orders; special orders from new or small vendors carry higher risk of non-delivery; deposit protects BuildRight but customer dissatisfaction from failed orders is costly
- **Specification errors**: customer-provided specifications that are inaccurate (wrong dimensions, wrong material) result in receiving an item the customer rejects; the product is then stranded in store inventory; mitigated by encouraging customers to bring physical samples or photos for matching
- **Unclaimed orders consuming backroom space**: at 15–25 special orders/store/month with an average 7–14 day hold period, 10–15 items are staged in the backroom at any time; during peak periods this strains limited backroom capacity
- **Pricing margin erosion**: Associates may under-price special orders due to difficulty in sourcing cost estimation, especially for import items with variable landed costs; standard 35–45% margin target must be enforced at Category Manager approval (Step 4)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-create of "create Special Order Request" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "check existing vendor master for suppliers of the product category" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "prepares quotation for customer" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: transparent lead time communication at quotation (Step 3) and suggesting in-stock alternatives where possible; operational: encouraging customers to bring physical samples or photos for matching

### Staffing Implication

- **CSR**: ~15–25 orders/store/month × 30 min average per order lifecycle = ~7.5–12.5 hours/month; absorbed within existing CSR duties
- **Category Manager**: ~10–15 min per special order approval × 3,000–5,000/month chain-wide = ~500–1,250 hours/month distributed across the ~15 Category Managers and Buyers = ~35–85 hours/month each; absorbed but significant during peak construction season
- **No incremental headcount** at current volumes

### Time Estimate

- Per special order lifecycle: customer request (15 min) + sourcing (1–4 hours) + quotation (30 min) + order placement (30 min) + weekly tracking (10 min × 4–12 weeks) + goods receipt (30 min) + pickup (15 min) = **3–8 hours over 2–12 weeks**
- Monthly chain-wide: ~3,000–5,000 orders × 4 hours average = ~12,000–20,000 hours/month (distributed across 200 stores)

---

## W1511. Store-Level Community Engagement, Barangay Relations & Local Partnership Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly community engagement calendar; barangay official visit or request; LGU partnership opportunity; community event invitation; store opening in new area |
| **Frequency** | ~4–8 community touchpoints per store per month; 1 formal LGU/barangay meeting per quarter per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~6 touchpoints/month = ~1,200 community engagement events/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Assistant Store Manager, Sales Associates, Regional Operations Manager, Marketing Manager, HR Manager |

### Background

In the Philippines, retail store success is deeply tied to community relationships. Each BuildRight Depot store operates within a specific barangay (the smallest local government unit) and must maintain positive relationships with the barangay council, barangay captain, LGU business permit office, local police, fire station, and community organizations. Unlike W1106 (community building materials donation, which is a specific transactional workflow), this workflow covers the broader, ongoing community engagement strategy: barangay relations, local partnership development, community event participation, and store reputation management at the local level. For a chain with 200 stores across diverse Philippine communities — from urban Metro Manila barangays to rural Mindanao municipalities — effective community engagement directly impacts business permit renewals, local customer loyalty, word-of-mouth marketing, and store security cooperation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Barangay relationship maintenance**: Store Manager maintains ongoing relationship with barangay officials: (a) attend barangay council meetings when store-related agenda items arise (business permit, traffic flow, parking, noise complaints, waste disposal), (b) invite barangay captain to store events (grand opening, anniversary, community build), (c) respond to barangay-issued memorandums or directives (clearance sales notifications, community tax certificates, special barangay permits for outdoor events), (d) support barangay initiatives: donate materials for community projects per W1106, sponsor barangay sports leagues or fiestas | Store Manager | Regional Ops Mgr | 2–4 hours/month |
| 2 | **Local partnership development**: Store Manager identifies and cultivates partnerships with local organizations: (a) local contractors' association — invite members to trade account program per W1269, offer store tour of new products, host monthly "Contractor Morning" with free coffee and product demos, (b) local hardware stores (smaller competitors) — explore referral partnerships where small hardware stores direct customers to BuildRight for items they don't carry (tiles, appliances, power tools) in exchange for reciprocal referrals for small items, (c) local schools and churches — offer bulk material pricing for renovation projects, (d) local real estate developers — establish project supply agreements per W162 for new housing developments in the area | Store Manager / Sales Associate | Regional Ops Mgr | 4–8 hours/month |
| 3 | **Community event participation**: Store participates in local community events: (a) LGU-sponsored events: town fiestas, foundation days, trade fairs — set up BuildRight booth with product displays, free project estimation per W148, loyalty program sign-up, (b) community build events: partner with LGU or NGOs for community projects (school repairs, public market renovation) — donate materials per W1106 and provide volunteer labor from store staff, (c) DIY workshops per W147 hosted at the store — open to community members, promote via barangay announcement, (d) store-sponsored events: annual customer appreciation day, free safety seminar for local construction workers | Store Manager / Sales Associates | Regional Ops Mgr / Marketing Mgr | 8–16 hours/event (quarterly) |
| 4 | **Local permit & regulatory relationship management**: Store Manager ensures positive relationship with local regulatory offices: (a) LGU Business Permit and Licensing Office (BPLO): maintain timely renewal per W460, attend any required hearings or inspections, (b) Bureau of Fire Protection (BFP): annual fire safety inspection compliance per W1051, invite BFP to conduct fire safety awareness event at store, (c) local DENR office: ensure environmental compliance per W502 and DOC-007 (hazardous waste), (d) barangay health office: coordinate on pest control per W503 and sanitation standards, (e) local PNP (Philippine National Police): establish direct contact with local police chief for security coordination per W471, report suspicious activities promptly | Store Manager | Regional Ops Mgr | 2–3 hours/month |
| 5 | **Customer loyalty community building**: Build local customer loyalty through community-centric activities: (a) support local sports teams with BuildRight-sponsored jerseys or equipment, (b) sponsor local job fairs — BuildRight as employer of choice in the community, host recruitment drives per W172, (c) "BuildRight Community Hero" program: recognize local contractors, teachers, or community leaders with special discounts or store recognition, (d) sponsor or participate in barangay disaster preparedness drills — position BuildRight as a resource for emergency construction materials, (e) partner with local TESDA offices for skills training workshops at the store per W600 contractor pipeline development | Store Manager / HR / Marketing | Regional Ops Mgr | 4–8 hours/month |
| 6 | **Community feedback & issue resolution**: Monitor and respond to community feedback: (a) Store Manager listens to community concerns relayed through Sales Associates, security guards, or direct feedback (noise, traffic, drainage, construction debris), (b) resolve issues proactively before they escalate to formal barangay complaints, (c) if community issue cannot be resolved at store level: escalate to Regional Ops Manager with proposed resolution, (d) track community feedback themes monthly and include in store operations review per W575 | Store Manager | Regional Ops Mgr | 2–4 hours/month |
| 7 | **Quarterly community engagement review**: Regional Ops Manager reviews community engagement effectiveness across stores: (a) store-level community engagement activity log (events attended, partnerships formed, LGU interactions), (b) business permit renewal status — stores with strong community relationships experience faster permit processing, (c) local customer acquisition: % of new loyalty sign-ups attributed to community events, (d) community feedback themes and resolution rate, (e) identify stores needing additional community engagement support (new stores, stores in areas with competitive pressure) | Regional Ops Mgr | VP Store Operations | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Community engagement activity log per store (event type, attendees, costs, outcomes)
- LGU/Barangay contact directory per store location
- Business permit renewal tracking per NFR-017
- Community event cost tracking and budget allocation
- Loyalty sign-up attribution to community events
- Partnership and referral tracking (trade account pipeline per W1269)
- Store-level community feedback log linked to customer complaint system per W41

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Community engagement is unstructured and personality-dependent**: Store Managers with strong interpersonal skills build effective community relationships; weaker Store Managers neglect community engagement, leading to permit delays, community complaints, and competitive vulnerability; mitigated by quarterly review (Step 7) and standardized engagement calendar
- **Donation requests are constant and difficult to decline**: barangay officials, schools, churches, and NGOs continuously request material donations; declining risks relationship damage; approving all requests is financially unsustainable; mitigated by annual community engagement budget per store (PHP 50,000–100,000/year) managed by Regional Ops Manager
- **LGU corruption risks**: some LGU officials may expect "facilitation fees" for business permit renewals or inspections; BuildRight's anti-corruption policy prohibits facilitation payments; Store Managers must navigate these situations without damaging relationships; Legal Counsel provides guidance per VS-22 compliance protocols; mitigated by quarterly review (Step 7) and standardized engagement calendar and operational: annual community engagement budget per store (PHP
- **Community event ROI is difficult to measure**: the direct revenue impact of sponsoring a barangay fiesta or hosting a contractor morning is hard to quantify; mitigated by tracking loyalty sign-ups, trade account conversions, and sales lift in the period following community events
- **Political neutrality**: in Philippine local politics, store sponsorship of community events may be interpreted as political support; Store Managers must maintain political neutrality and avoid exclusive partnerships with specific political factions


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-issue of "issued memorandums or directives" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "account program per W1269, offer store tour of new products, host monthly…" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-inspect of "Local permit & regulatory relationship management" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: quarterly review (Step 7) and standardized engagement calendar; operational: annual community engagement budget per store (PHP 50,000–100,000/year) managed by Regional Ops Manager; operational: tracking loyalty sign-ups, trade account conversions, and sales lift in the period following community events

### Staffing Implication

- **Store Manager**: ~15–30 hours/month on community engagement activities; significant portion of Store Manager's non-operations time
- **Assistant Store Manager**: ~5–10 hours/month supporting community events and partnerships
- **Sales Associates**: ~4–8 hours/quarter on community events (booth setup, product demos)
- **No incremental headcount**; community engagement is an integral part of the Store Manager role

### Time Estimate

- Per store per month: ~15–30 hours across all community engagement activities
- Per community event: ~8–16 hours (planning + execution + follow-up)
- Chain-wide: ~3,000–6,000 hours/month across 200 stores
- **Annual: ~36,000–72,000 hours/year chain-wide**

## W1537. Store-Level Parking Lot & Exterior Area Daily Operations, Customer Vehicle Flow & Security Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily store opening; or special event (sale weekend, delivery truck arrival) |
| **Frequency** | Daily (200 stores) |
| **Volume** | ~200–400 customer vehicles per store per day; ~2–3 delivery trucks per store per week (per profile §7.3) |
| **Owner** | Assistant Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Assistant Store Manager, Maintenance/Utility, Receiving Clerk, Security Guard |

### Background

BuildRight Depot stores are big-box warehouse format (8,000–15,000 sqm selling area) with large exterior areas including customer parking, lumber yard access, loading zones, and delivery truck receiving areas. With the majority of customers arriving by vehicle and purchasing bulky/heavy items (cement, lumber, tiles, appliances), the parking lot and exterior areas are a critical part of the customer experience. Poor exterior area management leads to traffic congestion, customer frustration, vehicle damage, safety incidents, and security vulnerabilities.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Morning exterior inspection**: Maintenance/Utility conducts pre-opening walk-through of parking lot, loading zone, and receiving area — check for overnight damage, debris, standing water (rainy season), proper lighting, signage visibility, speed bump condition; reports issues to Assistant Store Manager | Maintenance/Utility | Assistant Store Manager | 20 min |
| 2 | **Traffic flow setup**: For peak days (sale weekends, paydays — W578), Assistant Store Manager deploys traffic cones, directional signage, and additional parking guidance; coordinates with Security Guard for vehicle entry/exit flow management; activates overflow parking if needed | Assistant Store Manager | Store Manager | 15 min (peak days) |
| 3 | **Delivery truck staging**: Receiving Clerk manages delivery truck queue in designated receiving area; trucks directed to appropriate dock door based on merchandise type; customer vehicles separated from delivery truck traffic for safety | Receiving Clerk | Assistant Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 4 | **Customer loading zone management**: Designated loading zone near lumber yard exit for customers loading bulky purchases; Assistant Store Manager ensures loading zone is clear of abandoned vehicles (15-minute limit); Sales Associates assist with loading assistance per W950 | Assistant Store Manager / Sales Associate | Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 5 | **Security patrol**: Security Guard conducts periodic exterior patrol — parking lot crime prevention (vehicle break-in deterrence), suspicious activity monitoring, perimeter fence check; CCTV exterior cameras monitored from security station | Security Guard | Store Manager | Every 2 hours |
| 6 | **Rainy season operations**: During Philippine rainy season (June–October), Maintenance/Utility deploys wet floor mats at store entrances, canopy umbrellas at cart return areas, additional drainage checks for parking lot flooding; Assistant Store Manager activates one-way traffic flow if parking lot partially flooded | Maintenance/Utility | Assistant Store Manager | As needed |
| 7 | **End-of-day exterior close**: Security Guard verifies all customer vehicles have departed; locks exterior gates; checks for abandoned items in parking lot; activates overnight security lighting and CCTV; reports any incidents or damage discovered | Security Guard | Assistant Store Manager | 20 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Parking lot incident reporting integrated with store operations dashboard (W1537.1)
- Delivery truck scheduling integration with receiving workflow (W1537.3)
- Security incident reporting integrated with loss prevention system (W37, W538)
- Weather alert integration for rainy season preparedness (W1537.6)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Parking lot flooding**: Philippine heavy rains can flood unpaved or poorly drained parking areas within minutes, blocking customer access and causing vehicle damage
- **Vehicle break-ins**: Customer vehicles loaded with purchased construction materials are targets for theft; visible security presence and CCTV deterrence are essential
- **Loading zone congestion**: Customers loading bulky items (lumber, cement, tiles) can block the loading zone for extended periods, causing traffic backup and customer frustration


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "check for overnight damage, debris, standing water" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System workflow orchestration of "coordinates with Security Guard for vehicle entry/exit flow management" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-monitor of "suspicious activity monitoring, perimeter fence check" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Time Estimate
- Morning inspection: 20 min
- Peak day traffic setup: 15 min
- Ongoing management: ~2–3 hours/day across all exterior operations
- End-of-day close: 20 min
- **Total per store per day: ~3–4 hours**

### Staffing Implication
- Maintenance/Utility (1 per store): exterior maintenance is ~30% of role
- Security Guard (vendor-provided): exterior patrol is part of standard duties
- No incremental headcount

## W5505. Concession Item Catalog, Barcode & Price-Label Onboarding & Governance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | (a) Concessionaire agreement execution (W177 steps 1–2); (b) new or changed concession item submitted by the concessionaire (new menu item, vending facing change, packaging/size change); (c) barcode change request on an existing concession item; (d) annual catalog reconciliation per agreement |
| **Frequency** | ~30–80 new-item requests per month chain-wide plus 1–2 seasonal catalog refreshes per agreement per year; barcode changes occasional (~20–50/month chain-wide); annual reconciliation ~200–600 agreements/year |
| **Volume** | ~200–600 active concessionaire agreements carrying typically 25–50 sellable items each (cafe menus, snack counters, vending facing banks; a few large multi-machine operators reach ~200) → ~8,000–15,000 concession SKUs chain-wide (agreement count and catalog size vary inversely: ~40 items/agreement at 200 agreements, ~25 at 600); legacy agreements on the single throughput SKU (W177 step 3) migrate at renewal |
| **Owner** | Master Data Manager |
| **Participants** | Concessionaire, Store Manager, Merchandising Ops & Master Data (VS-29), Category Manager (adjacency check), IT (portal & label infrastructure), Finance (AR — fee events per W5507) |

### Background

W177 tracks commission-based concessionaire sales through a single "Non-Inventory" throughput SKU, which supports billing but not item-level pricing, labeling, or barcode integrity. This workflow establishes the item-level concession catalog beneath W177: every sellable concession item becomes a first-class item-master record owned by the concessionaire (not BuildRight merchandise, and not consignment inventory per W23 — the goods remain the concessionaire's property and never enter BuildRight inventory valuation), with governed barcodes and compliant price labels. It is the in-store analog of the marketplace seller catalog (W3140) and the foundation on which concessionaire self-service pricing (W5506) and service-fee billing (W5507) run.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Catalog intake**: Concessionaire submits items through the concessionaire portal (or a structured file for first load): description, size/UOM, VAT-inclusive opening price, VAT class (food/non-food/service), proposed barcode if any, serving/facing photo; incomplete submissions are returned automatically with the missing fields listed | Concessionaire | Master Data Manager | 5 min/item (portal); 1–2 hrs first-load file |
| 2 | **Item-master creation**: Merchandising Ops & Master Data create the record in the concession item namespace with a concessionaire-owner flag and agreement link (W117); the record is distinct from BuildRight-owned SKUs (W252): no cost, no inventory valuation, no replenishment — price and description are concessionaire-controlled fields pending W5506 approval, tax and category flags are BuildRight-controlled; POS-ring behavior and commission calculation (W177 step 4) attach at item level | Master Data Analyst | Master Data Manager | 10 min/item |
| 3 | **Barcode governance**: (a) concessionaire GS1 barcodes are accepted only after a uniqueness check against the full BuildRight barcode space (W311) — a collision with any BuildRight or other concessionaire barcode blocks activation; (b) items without a compliant barcode receive a BuildRight internal barcode from the concession range, following the custom-barcode pattern of W168/W169; (c) a **barcode change on an existing item** follows the same governance: new code verified unique, old code delinked with an effective date, and a label-reprint task auto-raised (step 4) — the old code stops ringing at the effective cutover, never before the new label is confirmed affixed (sequencing per W5506 step 5) | Master Data Analyst | Master Data Manager | 10–15 min/item; 30 min per collision investigation |
| 4 | **Price-label ownership decision** (recorded on the agreement at setup, per item class): (a) *BuildRight-printed* — concession items join the store label batch cycle (W181 pattern) on BuildRight template/label stock; or (b) *concessionaire-printed* — concessionaire prints on the BuildRight-approved template (item, price incl. VAT, barcode, concessionaire brand) and affixes, with photo confirmation captured in the portal. Shelf-sold items must carry a compliant price tag per RA 7394 (Consumer Act) before any sale; vending-machine face labels follow the machine's own label strip but must match the POS price file exactly | Store Manager / Concessionaire | Master Data Manager | 30 min per agreement (decision + template setup) |
| 5 | **POS activation**: approved items with confirmed labels activate in the price file; central push reaches POS terminals within 60 seconds (continuous price push per the POS architecture); offline terminals apply on reconnect with a stale-price flag | System / IT | Master Data Manager | Automated |
| 6 | **Change & retirement handling**: description/UOM/photo changes follow steps 1–2; item retirement (menu removal, facing exit) deactivates the SKU at a cutover date after the label is confirmed removed; retirements feed the W177 monthly commission reconciliation so removed items stop accruing | Concessionaire / Master Data Analyst | Store Manager | 10 min/item |
| 7 | **Annual catalog reconciliation**: per agreement, Master Data reconciles the active catalog against the agreement scope and the physical floor/machines (with Store Manager); ghost SKUs (agreement-ended items still active) are purged chain-wide — the concessionaire-exit variant runs through W3144-style offboarding and W62 termination where applicable; results feed the W5507 recovery review | Master Data Analyst / Store Manager | Master Data Manager | ~1 hr/agreement/year |

### System Touchpoints
- Concession item-master namespace with concessionaire-owner flag, agreement link (W117), and BuildRight-controlled tax/category flags vs. concessionaire-controlled price/description fields (item governance per VS-29)
- Concessionaire portal catalog upload with field validation and automatic return of incomplete submissions
- Barcode uniqueness service against the BuildRight-wide barcode register (W311); internal-barcode assignment from the concession range (W168/W169 pattern)
- Label template service + store label-batch integration (W181 pattern) with portal photo-confirmation for concessionaire-printed labels
- Continuous POS item/price push with stale-price flag on offline terminals
- Chain-wide SKU purge function for agreement exit (feed to W177 settlement and W62 termination)
- Chargeable-event logging hooks to W5507 (item adds beyond free allowance, barcode changes, template/custom stock)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Barcode collision mis-ring risk**: a concessionaire barcode colliding with a BuildRight SKU rings BuildRight merchandise at the concession stand (or vice versa), corrupting both parties' sales data; mitigated by the mandatory uniqueness gate before activation (step 3) — collisions block, never warn
- **Ghost-SKU drift**: concession items left active after an agreement ends keep ringing sales that accrue commission to a defunct agreement; mitigated by the annual reconciliation and exit purge (step 7)
- **RA 7394 tag exposure**: an untagged or mispriced concession shelf item exposes BuildRight (as the store operator) to consumer-act findings; mitigated by the no-sale-before-confirmed-label rule (steps 4–5) and W667/W69 audit sweeps that include concession items
- **VAT-class error**: food items flagged non-food (or vice versa) misstate the VAT split in the monthly concessionaire settlement; mitigated by BuildRight-controlled tax flags with food/non-food validation at creation (step 2)
- **Template drift on concessionaire-printed labels**: concessionaires print on their own stock to save cost, drifting from the approved template; mitigated by photo-confirmation review and the right to withdraw self-print privilege (reverting to BuildRight printing, fee-chargeable per W5507)

### Automation Opportunity
- Portal auto-validation of catalog submissions with automatic return of incomplete rows (supports Step 1).
- Barcode uniqueness auto-check at submission, blocking collisions before item creation (supports Step 3).
- Auto-raise of label-reprint tasks and fee events on barcode change (supports Steps 3–4).
- Ghost-SKU auto-sweep on agreement end date (supports Step 7).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- CTL-195 (ensure planogram & price-tag compliance — concession items join the shelf-label price-accuracy audit population)
- operational: barcode uniqueness gate blocks activation on any collision; no sale before confirmed label affixture

### Time Estimate
- First catalog load: ~1–2 hrs per agreement (file-based) + 10 min/item master-data creation; steady-state adds ~30–80/month chain-wide × ~15 min = 8–20 hrs/month; barcode changes ~20–50/month × 15 min plus collision investigations; annual reconciliation ~1 hr × 200–600 agreements = 200–600 hrs/year concentrated in the Master Data team (4 staff) and Store Managers — absorbed; no incremental headcount

### Cross-references
- W177 (parent agreement & settlement), W117 (lease/agreement record), W62 (termination), W23 (consignment — explicitly distinct: concession goods never enter BuildRight inventory), W252 (BuildRight item master), W296 (service & non-stock item master), W311 (barcode/GTIN governance), W168/W169 (internal custom-barcode pattern), W181 (label print cycle), W667/W69 (price verification & audit), W5506 (price changes on this catalog), W5507 (fees chargeable on these events), W3140/W3144 (marketplace seller-catalog & offboarding analogs), W5B (POS selling)
- VS-29 (master data), VS-08 (POS), VS-95 (marketplace self-service analog), VS-22/RA 7394 (price-tag compliance)

## W5506. Concessionaire Self-Service Price Change Request, Approval & Store-Level Propagation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Concessionaire submits a price change through the concessionaire portal (cost increase, menu change, promo price, error correction); or requests an emergency rollback |
| **Frequency** | ~150–400 price-change requests per month chain-wide (food-input costs, seasonal menus, promo bursts), averaging ~7–10 items per request → ~1,000–4,000 item-level price changes/month |
| **Volume** | All ~8,000–15,000 concession SKUs (W5505) are eligible; legacy single-SKU agreements (W177 step 3) are out of scope until migrated |
| **Owner** | Pricing Analyst |
| **Participants** | Concessionaire (requester), Store Manager (single-store approval), Category Manager (multi-store/chain-wide approval), Stock Associate (label swap), IT (price push), Finance (commission rate unchanged — no role in price decision) |

### Background

Concession prices belong to the concessionaire, but BuildRight owns the checkout: prices ring on BuildRight POS, on BuildRight label stock, inside BuildRight stores — so every concession price change must pass a BuildRight approval gate and then propagate through exactly one unambiguous sequence. This workflow defines that sequence end-to-end: portal request → automated guardrail validation → approve/reject with SLA → **label-first, price-second cutover** (a new price never activates at POS before the matching shelf tag is confirmed affixed — RA 7394 forbids selling above the posted tag) → post-cutover audit. It is the in-store analog of marketplace seller-initiated promotions review (W3152) and executes against the same effective-dated price master governed by W289/W40.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Submission**: Concessionaire drafts the change in the portal: item(s) from the W5505 catalog, new VAT-inclusive price, requested effective date (≥ 3 business days out; same/next-day expedite available per the W5507 fee schedule), and reason code. Drafts have **no system effect** — the POS price and label stock remain untouched until step 5 | Concessionaire | — | 5 min/request |
| 2 | **Automated guardrail validation** (seconds, blocking): (a) within agreement guardrails — max change % per request and absolute floor if agreed in the contract; (b) undercut check — new price more than X% below a comparable BuildRight SKU in the adjacent category flags for Category Manager review (advisory, not blocking); (c) DTI price-freeze check — if the item is on the frozen list (RA 7581, W468), the request is rejected automatically for the freeze duration; (d) VAT math — portal-entered gross price and implied net reconcile to the item's VAT class (W5505 step 2). Failed validations return to the concessionaire with reasons; passed requests queue for approval | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Approval decision** (SLA: 2 business days, auto-reminder at breach): (a) *single-store items* → Store Manager approves/rejects in the portal (rejection requires a written reason, returned to the concessionaire for rework); (b) *multi-store/chain-wide items* (e.g., a vending operator across 40 stores) → Pricing Analyst endorses, Category Manager approves; (c) *auto-approval* is permitted only when ALL hold: change ≤ ±10%, no barcode/label-format change, item not flagged by the undercut check, requester has no active billing dispute (W5507 step 4). Approval is recorded with approver identity and timestamp | Store Manager / Pricing Analyst / Category Manager | Store Manager (single-store) or Category Manager (chain-wide) | 10 min/manual review; ~40–60% auto-approved |
| 4 | **Scheduling**: on approval the system writes an effective-dated price record to the price master (W289 governance); cutover convention: **06:00 store-local on the effective date** (same start-of-business convention as W40 steps 6–8); the old price stays live until cutover | System | Pricing Analyst | Automated |
| 5 | **Label-first propagation** (the binding sequence — no step may run early): (a) **T-1 day 16:00** — label batch releases: BuildRight-printed items join the store's label print batch (W181 pattern); concessionaire-printed items get a portal task due by T-1 close; (b) **T-1 close → effective 06:00** — label swap executed and scan-confirmed (handheld scan of the new tag, or portal photo-confirmation with timestamp for concessionaire-printed); (c) **effective 06:00** — POS price-file activation pushes within 60 seconds to online terminals; offline terminals defer activation and fly a stale-price flag until reconnect (never applying a price whose label was confirmed but whose push lagged); (d) **05:30 hold rule** — if the label swap is NOT confirmed by 05:30 on the effective date for any store, that store's activation auto-holds (old price keeps ringing — the customer can never be charged above the posted tag per RA 7394) and the exception escalates to the Store Manager for same-day resolution; (e) vending machines: machine-side price update pushed by the concessionaire operator via telemetry where connected, else manual update with photo confirmation, by the same 05:30 deadline | Stock Associate / Concessionaire / System | Store Manager | 15–30 min/store/event (label swap); automated elsewhere |
| 6 | **Barcode-change cutover**: when the price change rides on a barcode change (W5505 step 3c), the new barcode activates in the same 06:00 cutover and only after the new label is confirmed; the old barcode then returns "not found" with a 7-day courtesy alias that rings the new price and alerts the cashier to update the tag | System | Master Data Manager | Automated (alias monitored) |
| 7 | **Post-cutover verification**: W667 daily price verification sweeps concession SKUs on every price-change cutover day (not just random sampling); W69 price compliance audits include concession items in scope; a POS-vs-shelf mismatch triggers immediate re-print per W181 step 5 and, where the customer was charged above the posted tag, the lower-of-two price rule and a goodwill adjustment per W101 | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15 min/day (absorbed in W667) |
| 8 | **Audit trail & settlement feed**: every request retains requester, approver, validation results, timestamps, old/new price, and label-confirmation evidence for 10 years (BIR retention); approved changes feed the W177 monthly commission calculation at the new price from the cutover minute, and changes beyond the free allowance log a fee event to W5507 | System | Pricing Analyst | Automated |
| 9 | **Emergency rollback**: concessionaire or BuildRight may request a same-day rollback of an approved change (pricing error, supplier cost reversal); rollback follows the identical label-first sequence compressed to same-day (print → swap-confirm → activate) with the expedite fee chargeable per W5507; rollback of a price DECREASE may activate immediately (charging less than the posted tag is never an RA 7394 violation) with labels following within 24 hours | Pricing Analyst / Store Manager | Store Manager | 1–2 hrs/event |

### System Touchpoints
- Concessionaire portal request queue with draft/pending/approved/rejected states and written rejection reasons
- Guardrail engine: per-agreement change limits & floors, undercut check vs. BuildRight SKU comparables, W468 price-freeze status hook, VAT-class math
- Approval workflow with SLA timers, auto-reminders, auto-approval rules, and approver identity capture (Store Manager single-store; Category Manager chain-wide)
- Effective-dated price master records (W289) with 06:00 cutover scheduling
- Label print-batch release at T-1 16:00 with handheld scan-confirmation (W181 pattern) and portal photo-confirmation (timestamped) for concessionaire-printed tags
- Continuous POS price push (60-second activation) with offline stale-price deferral; 05:30 activation hold + Store Manager escalation on unconfirmed swaps
- Vending telemetry / machine-side price push with photo fallback
- Barcode alias ring-with-alert during the 7-day post-change window (W5505 step 3c)
- 10-year audit trail (BIR) feeding W177 commission calc and W5507 fee events

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Posted-price breach (RA 7394)**: activating a POS price above the shelf tag is a consumer-act violation and an LP/Audit finding; mitigated by the binding label-first sequence and the 05:30 auto-hold rule (step 5) — the system structurally cannot strand a store with price-ahead-of-tag
- **Approval bottleneck**: a 2-day SLA across 200–600 agreements can throttle concessionaire repricing and sour partner relations; mitigated by guardrail auto-approval (step 3c) clearing the ~40–60% routine volume
- **Freeze-window violation (RA 7581)**: repricing a basic-necessity concession item (e.g., bottled water, instant noodles) during a DTI price freeze attracts penalties; mitigated by the automatic freeze-status rejection (step 2c)
- **Category undercutting**: a concessionaire pricing coffee or snacks far below BuildRight's own adjacent category cannibalizes BuildRight margin; mitigated by the undercut flag routing to Category Manager review (step 2b) — advisory by design, since concession pricing is the concessionaire's commercial right within the agreement
- **Label-evidence falsification**: reused or pre-dated photos defeat the label-first guarantee for concessionaire-printed tags; mitigated by timestamped capture in the portal and audit re-verification during W69, with self-print privilege withdrawal as the penalty
- **Offline-terminal gap**: a terminal that reconnects after cutover could ring the stale old price; mitigated by stale-price deferral — the terminal flags and requires supervisor acknowledgment rather than silently ringing either price

### Automation Opportunity
- Guardrail auto-validation and auto-return of failed submissions (supports Step 2).
- Auto-approval within ±10%/no-format-change/no-dispute rules (supports Step 3).
- Auto-scheduled label-batch release at T-1 16:00 and 60-second POS activation at cutover (supports Step 5).
- 05:30 auto-hold and Store Manager escalation on unconfirmed label swaps (supports Step 5).
- Auto-logging of W5507 fee events on approval and of the W177 commission feed at cutover (supports Step 8).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- CTL-241 (ensure controlled execution — Pricing & Promotions (PA-01.2))
- operational: label-first sequencing with 05:30 activation hold — no POS price activates before confirmed label affixture; freeze-status rejection per W468

### Time Estimate
- ~150–400 requests/month × 10 min manual review for the ~40–60% not auto-approved = 10–40 hrs/month absorbed by the 4 Pricing Analysts and Store Managers; label swaps 15–30 min/store/event absorbed in the W181 morning routine; W667 concession sweeps add ~15 min/day per store with a same-day cutover (not every store, every day); no incremental headcount

### Cross-references
- W5505 (catalog the changes run against), W5507 (fees on expedite/excess changes), W177 (agreement, commission settlement), W40 (BuildRight price-change execution — same 06:00 cutover & price-history conventions), W289 (pricing master governance), W181 (label print/swap/verify cycle), W667/W69 (price verification & compliance audit), W468 (DTI price freeze RA 7581), W63 (shelf label generation), W311 (barcode governance for step 6), W101 (customer refund/goodwill on overcharge), W473 (e-invoicing integrity downstream), W3152 (marketplace seller-promotion approval analog), W3142 (self-service portal analog), W5B (POS ring), W553 (checkout price-error correction)
- VS-01.2 (pricing execution), VS-08 (POS), VS-29 (master data), VS-22 (RA 7394 / RA 7581 compliance)

## W5507. Concession Service-Fee Billing & Cost Recovery for Labels, Barcode Changes & Admin Services

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | (a) Chargeable service event auto-logged from W5505/W5506 (label print run, barcode change, item add beyond free allowance, expedited or excess price change, custom label stock); (b) monthly concessionaire billing run (W177 step 4) |
| **Frequency** | Continuous event logging; ~200–600 concessionaire invoices per month carrying a service-fee section; ~300–800 chargeable events per month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | ~5–10% of invoices attract a dispute (~15–50 disputes/month) |
| **Owner** | AR Supervisor |
| **Participants** | AR Clerk, Concessionaire, Store Manager (first-line dispute), Master Data Analyst (event evidence), Finance (Controller — posting), Legal (fee-schedule enforceability at contracting) |

### Background

Executing catalog, label, and price-change services for concessionaires (W5505/W5506) consumes BuildRight labor, label stock, and print capacity. This workflow makes those services billable to the concessionaire on a contracted per-event fee schedule, with transparent evidence, a dispute window, and clean VAT/invoicing treatment — recovered either as a line on the monthly W177 invoice or, for agreements where BuildRight is the collection agent, netted from the concessionaire's gross sales before remittance. It follows the chargeback-recovery pattern of W1614 (vendor quality chargebacks) and the settlement-transparency pattern of W3153 (marketplace seller payouts).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Fee-schedule setup at contracting**: Legal/Finance record the schedule on the agreement (W177 step 2, W117 record): free allowances (e.g., 1 price change per item per month; 10 item adds per year; standard label stock) and per-event fees (label print per tag; barcode change per SKU; expedited same-day price change per event; excess price change per item; custom label stock at cost + margin; post-exit catalog clean-out). Versioned with effective dates so events bill at the schedule in force on the event date | Legal / Finance | Controller | 1 hr/agreement |
| 2 | **Event capture (no manual billing entry)**: every chargeable event is auto-logged at source with evidence — item/SKU, requester, portal request ID, approval ID, print-run count, timestamp (from W5505 steps 2–4, 7 and W5506 steps 3, 5, 9); events are provisional until the underlying service completes (e.g., a rejected price change logs no fee) | System | AR Supervisor | Automated |
| 3 | **Monthly aggregation**: the W177 billing run (step 4) gains a "Service Fees" section itemizing events by type with evidence links, alongside Rent + Utilities + Commission; for collection-agent agreements, service fees may instead net against the concessionaire's gross sales in the same statement, shown gross-then-net | AR Clerk | Controller | +15 min/agreement/month (on top of W177's 30 min) |
| 4 | **Statement & dispute window**: the concessionaire sees the itemized statement in the portal 5 business days before invoice finalization; disputes are raised in-portal and route first to the Store Manager (operational facts) then Finance (rate/application); resolution adjusts the invoice before posting; an unresolved dispute credits the contested line per W101 and escalates to the agreement-level relationship review (W177) — and an active unresolved dispute suspends that concessionaire's auto-approval privilege (W5506 step 3c) | Concessionaire / Store Manager / AR Clerk | AR Supervisor | 20 min per dispute (~15–50/month) |
| 5 | **Accounting & compliance**: service fees post as service revenue (distinct from rental income and commission income — each carries its own VAT characterization per BIR rules); invoicing follows the BIR e-invoicing path (W473) with 10-year evidence retention; AR posting, aging, and collection follow W8/W108 | AR Clerk / Accountant | Controller | 10 min/agreement/month |
| 6 | **Quarterly recovery review**: Finance compares fee income by event type against actual service cost (label stock & print amortization from W181, Master Data labor hours, expedite overtime); persistent under-recovery triggers a fee-schedule change at renewal (W117) rather than mid-term unilateral increases (contract integrity); results feed the annual catalog reconciliation (W5505 step 7) and agreement profitability view | Finance Analyst | Controller | 2 hrs/quarter |

### System Touchpoints
- Fee-schedule master versioned on the agreement record (W117) with effective dating
- Chargeable-event log with evidence links (portal request ID, approval ID, print-run count, timestamps) — events provisional until service completion
- Monthly billing-engine extension of the W177 invoice: Service Fees section with line-item evidence links; gross-then-net statement variant for collection-agent agreements
- Portal statement view with 5-business-day dispute window, in-portal dispute cases, and auto-approval suspension flag (W5506 step 3c)
- Service-revenue GL accounts separate from rent/commission; VAT treatment per line type; BIR e-invoicing integration (W473); AR aging per W8/W108; credit-note path per W101
- Quarterly recovery analytics: fee income vs. service cost by event type

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Unenforceable-fee risk**: charging events not covered by the contracted schedule (or at mid-term increased rates) is uncollectible and damages partner trust; mitigated by schedule-versioned billing at the in-force rate and changes only at renewal (steps 1, 6)
- **Double-billing / phantom events**: an event logged but not executed (rejected change, unreached label) still on the invoice destroys portal credibility; mitigated by provisional-until-completed event status with automatic reversal (step 2)
- **Dispute escalation friction**: parking fee disputes inside the W177 rent negotiation cycles conflates operational services with lease terms; mitigated by the dedicated dispute window with Store-Manager-first routing (step 4)
- **VAT misclassification**: treating label-service fees as rental income (or netting VAT-able services against commission without gross-up) misstates output tax; mitigated by separate service-revenue accounts and per-line VAT treatment (step 5)
- **Recovery gap**: fees set below actual print/labor cost silently subsidize concessionaires; mitigated by the quarterly recovery review with renewal-time correction (step 6)

### Automation Opportunity
- Auto-logging of chargeable events at source with evidence links and provisional-until-completed status (supports Step 2).
- Auto-aggregation of the Service Fees section into the W177 monthly invoice (supports Step 3).
- Portal statement auto-release 5 days pre-final with in-portal dispute case creation (supports Step 4).
- Recovery analytics auto-computed from the event log and W181 print-run data (supports Step 6).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: fee-schedule contractual basis enforced by versioned effective dating; provisional event reversal before invoice final; dispute window precedes posting

### Time Estimate
- Setup ~1 hr/agreement at contracting; steady state +15 min/agreement/month billing (+~2–3 hrs/month chain-wide over W177's 100–300 hrs) plus ~20 min × 15–50 disputes; quarterly review 2 hrs — absorbed by AR team and Finance; label/print costs partially offset by fee income (tracked in step 6); no incremental headcount

### Cross-references
- W177 (parent billing cycle), W5505/W5506 (event sources), W117 (agreement record & renewal), W8/W108 (AR & collections), W101 (disputed-line credit), W473 (BIR e-invoicing), W1614 (vendor chargeback-recovery pattern), W3153 (marketplace settlement-transparency pattern), W181 (print cost basis), W27 (rebates — distinct: incentives, not cost recovery), W62 (exit clean-out fee event)
