## W5574. Concessionaire Connectivity Request, Approval & Independent-Circuit Governance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | (a) A concessionaire requests an independent internet circuit for its concession space — at application (W177 step 1), mid-term, or at agreement renewal; (b) a network or facilities review surfaces an unauthorized circuit or self-installed connectivity equipment in a concession space |
| **Frequency** | Rare per store — ~0.5–1.5 requests per store per year ≈ 100–300 requests/yr chain-wide across 200 stores; unauthorized-findings intake is event-driven |
| **Volume** | ~200–600 active concession agreements (W177); ~100–300 connectivity requests/yr chain-wide at ~0.5–1.5 per store per year; historically ~10–20% of requests clear the eligibility gate (~10–60 approvals/yr chain-wide), building an installed base of a few dozen independent circuits; every agreement carrying one is re-attested annually (step 7) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Concessionaire, Facilities Coordinator, Network Engineer (IT Infrastructure), IT Security Manager, Finance (AR), Legal, Concessionaire-Contracted ISP |

### Background

BuildRight's default posture is that store connectivity — including connectivity used by concession spaces — is a BuildRight-provided utility: procured chain-wide (W366), consumed and benchmarked as a utility (W111), and recovered inside the monthly concessionaire invoice (W177 step 4). This workflow governs the exception: a concessionaire wanting its own internet circuit may have one only where the bundled service cannot meet a documented regulatory, banking, or capacity requirement, and only under an isolation envelope that keeps the circuit — and everything it carries — entirely outside BuildRight's networks. The first alternative evaluated is always a concessionaire-segmented Wi-Fi offering on BuildRight's own link (W366 step 4), billed as a utility; an independent circuit is approved only when that alternative is technically insufficient. Commission integrity holds in either case: all concession sales ring on the BuildRight POS throughput SKU (W177 step 3, W5505), so BuildRight's commission calculation never depends on the concessionaire's connectivity choice.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Request intake & posture check**: Store Manager logs the request against the agreement (W117) and issues the default-posture notice — connectivity at BuildRight stores is a BuildRight-provided utility recovered in the monthly concessionaire invoice (W177 step 4, W111); an independent circuit is an exception requiring this workflow's approvals, and the concessionaire-segmented Wi-Fi alternative on the store's own link (W366 step 4) is offered first | Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min/request |
| 2 | **Eligibility & need review**: request proceeds only when the bundled service cannot meet a documented requirement — a dedicated payment or ATM link, a connection to a bank or government system mandated for the concessionaire's own regulated operations, or bandwidth the store link cannot carry without breaching the W366 QoS floors protecting RF guns and mPOS; convenience or cost alone is not eligibility; ineligible requests are declined with the bundled/segmented alternative documented on the case | Store Manager / Facilities Coordinator | Store Manager | 30–60 min/request |
| 3 | **Technical isolation review**: Network Engineer and IT Security Manager define the isolation envelope — circuit entry via a separate conduit or rise terminated in a concessionaire-owned enclosure; no physical or logical connection to any BuildRight network (corporate LAN, store Wi-Fi, POS/OT zones per W5418); DHCP service confined to the concessionaire's own equipment per the W5520 IPAM rules; no BuildRight credentials issued for any circuit-attached device | Network Engineer / IT Security Manager | IT Security Manager | 1 hr/request |
| 4 | **Facilities & works approval**: Facilities Coordinator approves the physical route (penetrations, pathway, exterior termination) per W47; exterior works (dish, antenna, façade penetration) additionally require landlord consent where BuildRight is itself a tenant (VS-42/VS-97 master-lease discipline) and LGU permits where applicable; execution only by licensed contractors from the approved list (W47), with a restoration obligation and a restoration holdback retained from the concessionaire's deposit | Facilities Coordinator | Facilities Coordinator | 1 hr/request (works 1–2 weeks elapsed) |
| 5 | **Contract addendum**: Legal and Finance execute the connectivity addendum to the agreement (recorded on W117): demarcation of responsibility (the circuit, its equipment and its support are exclusively the concessionaire's — BuildRight IT is not the helpdesk for it per W48 scope), liability and insurance requirements per VS-147, the restoration duty at exit, and the clause that all concession sales continue to ring on the BuildRight POS throughput SKU (W177 step 3, W5505) regardless of the concessionaire's own systems | Legal / Finance (AR) | Store Manager | 1–2 hrs/addendum |
| 6 | **Installation & verification**: ISP and contractor visits are scheduled under the vendor-representative access procedure (W581) with Store Manager escort per W71; on completion, IT Security verifies the isolation envelope (no crossover, no bridging, DHCP boundary honored) before the agreement's independent-circuit flag is set; any network-side accommodation such as a port-isolation rule rides W1409 change control | IT Security Manager / Store Manager | IT Security Manager | 2 hrs/verification |
| 7 | **Ongoing governance**: the independent-circuit flag lives on the agreement record; annual re-attestation rides the concession catalog reconciliation (W5505 step 7) — circuit still installed per the addendum, no crossovers surfaced by discovery (W5520 reconciliation), insurance current; findings suspend the flag pending re-verification | Store Manager / IT Security Manager | Store Manager | 15 min/agreement/yr |
| 8 | **Billing adjustment & exit decommissioning**: Finance removes the internet line from the monthly concessionaire invoice (W177 step 4; W111 allocation) from the addendum's effective date; at agreement end (W62) the exit walk-through verifies circuit cancellation by the concessionaire and removal of cabling and equipment before the restoration holdback is released; leftover cabling is removed at concessionaire cost | Finance (AR) / Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min billing adjustment; 1 hr exit walk-through |

### System Touchpoints
- Concessionaire agreement record connectivity addendum flag and demarcation note (agreement/lease module per W117)
- Request/approval case object with eligibility decision, isolation envelope, and approval trail (store-operations workflow queue)
- Network management: port-isolation/NAC rule on the concessionaire rise; SD-WAN/NMS annotation of the excluded circuit (W366)
- IPAM register excluded-range annotation and discovery-reconciliation exclusion list so concessionaire-space equipment does not flag as rogue (W5520)
- AR billing-line adjustment on the monthly concessionaire invoice (W177 step 4; utility allocation per W111)
- Contractor work order with restoration holdback on the deposit record (W47)
- Visitor/contractor access log entries for ISP technicians (W581; W71 escort rule)
- Exit decommissioning checklist task auto-raised on agreement termination (W62)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Crossover risk**: a concessionaire router or patch cable bridged into the store LAN introduces a rogue DHCP server that breaks store-to-DC routing (the W5520 IP-collision class); mitigated by the pre-activation isolation verification (step 6), the port-isolation rule, and the annual discovery-reconciliation sweep (step 7)
- **Shadow-device risk**: concessionaire equipment with vendor cloud links (its own POS, cameras, IoT sensors) reaching from its circuit toward BuildRight systems reproduces the W5421 shadow-OT class; mitigated by the no-credentials rule and the isolation verification before the flag is set
- **Commission leakage risk**: an independent link lets the concessionaire run its own selling or booking systems off-POS, starving the W177 commission calculation; mitigated by the addendum clause keeping every sale on the BuildRight throughput SKU under W5505 catalog governance, with W667/W69 sweeps extending to concession items
- **Demarcation-creep risk**: concession outage complaints for a circuit BuildRight neither owns nor monitors landing on BuildRight IT as W48 tickets; mitigated by the demarcation clause and the boundary note on the agreement record
- **Abandonment risk**: cancelled agreements leaving live circuits, dangling cabling, or roof and façade hardware (the W177 abandonment class, with added landlord exposure where a master lease governs the building); mitigated by the restoration holdback and the W62 exit walk-through gate before deposit release

### Automation Opportunity
- Request intake auto-attaches the default-posture notice and eligibility criteria and drafts the decline with the segmented-Wi-Fi alternative for non-qualifying asks (supports Steps 1–2).
- Discovery alert on an unregistered DHCP server in a concession space auto-opens an isolation-review case (supports Steps 3 and 7).
- Agreement-termination event auto-raises the exit decommissioning checklist and releases the restoration holdback only on walk-through sign-off (supports Step 8).

### Controls
- CTL-259 (ensure controlled execution — Store Daily Management (PA-07.1).)
- operational: no physical or logical crossing into BuildRight networks without IT Security verification; all concession sales continue on the BuildRight POS throughput SKU; restoration holdback released only on exit walk-through sign-off

### Time Estimate
- ~2.5 hrs handling per request (1–1.5 hrs intake and eligibility review + 1 hr decision and documentation) × ~100–300 requests/yr ≈ 250–750 hrs/yr chain-wide; approved requests add ~4 hrs install coordination and isolation verification per approval × ~10–60 approvals/yr ≈ 40–240 hrs/yr; annual re-attestation ~15 min per independent-circuit agreement × ~15–45 agreements ≈ 4–11 hrs/yr; exit walk-through 1 hr per departing flagged agreement (a handful per year). All absorbed: requests are rare per store (~0.5–1.5/yr) and handled inside existing Store Manager, Facilities, Network Engineering, IT Security and Legal routines; no incremental headcount

### Cross-references
- W177 (parent concession management & monthly billing), W5505/W5506/W5507 (concession catalog, pricing and fee machinery the circuit must not bypass), W117 (agreement record & addendum), W62 (termination & exit walk-through), W111 (utility bundle & allocation), W366 (store network & segmented Wi-Fi alternative), W5520 (IPAM/DHCP governance & discovery reconciliation), W5418/W5421 (IT/OT segmentation & connected-device onboarding gate), W1409 (network change control), W47 (facility works & restoration), W581 (vendor-representative access), W71 (physical security & escort), W48 (IT incident scope demarcation), W667/W69 (price-verification sweeps covering concession items)
- VS-42/VS-97 (lease & landlord/master-lease discipline), VS-147 (premises liability & insurance), VS-161 (third-party risk), VS-190 (IT/OT boundary ownership), VS-27 (network & security ownership)
